### 2. **Guidance + Default (Skill Description)**

- **Context:** Guidance in workflow prose; described as default not mandate
- **Enforcement:** Guidance; examples show this is principle-of-practice not hard constraint

### 3. **Canonical Rule Document**
- **File:** `/Users/anton/src/pde-skills/rules/linear-workflow.md`

### 4. **Reference Document (Type Playbooks)**

100	- **Type 4 (research / thesis-driven)** — knowledge-claim KR (committed) + critique-survivability KR (aspirational, lagging) + source-discipline KR (committed). Adoption KR (paying audience engagement, validated buyer interest) is a later-stage 4th — defer until critique-survivability has held. Readership of an undefended thesis is noise; "publish N essays" or "N readers" KRs are output, not defensibility. See Type 4 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`.
101	- **Type 5 (equity research tooling)** — pre-registration KR (committed) + calibration KR (aspirational, lagging) + hit-rate KR (aspirational, lagging). Decision-quality KR (per-call postmortem) optional 4th when the cycle window covers prior 12-month outcomes. Pre-registration is non-negotiable — every BUY/WATCH/AVOID call must be written to a dated, immutable artefact (cached `reports/TICKER_YYYYMMDD.json` + git history) before outcomes are visible, or the lagging KRs are ungradable. This is the Tetlock standard: calibration over a portfolio of pre-registered calls beats accuracy on any single name.
102	- **Type 6 (production / customer-facing)** — activation/retention/conversion KR (aspirational, lagging) + quality-pair KR (committed — NPS, p95 latency, error rate, or support volume per user) + telemetry-discipline KR (committed). Growth without a paired quality counterweight is the canonical failure mode — every value KR must have a paired quality KR. Telemetry must be in place before the cycle starts (no telemetry = no Type 6 KR); "ship feature X to 100% of users" and un-paired MAU/DAU KRs are Cagan's canonical output-disguised-as-outcome. See Type 6 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`.
103	
104	Then ask explicitly. Do not infer. Wait for a response before synthesising.
105	
106	- **Who is affected?** Which users, operators, or contexts does this problem touch?
107	- **What's the negative outcome if this isn't solved?** What task fails, what decision can't be made, what workflow breaks?
108	- **What 3 observable states would tell you it worked?** (Cap at 5; default 3 — per Wodtke.) Each one should be something a future agent can verify by looking at the system — a binary pass/fail, a fitness function firing, or a measurable delta. Avoid "improve X" / "better Y" language and avoid arbitrary "run N times" thresholds. **For all six types, propose the default mix from the playbook (Type 1: invocation-rate + decision-quality + anti-output guard; Type 2: correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden, with availability as an optional 4th; Type 3: first-shot correctness + coverage + use-log discipline, with domain-specific quality as an optional 4th — and never omit the use-log discipline KR for Type 3, and require the use-log location be named as a file path or directory in the KR's source field; Type 4: knowledge-claim + critique-survivability + source-discipline, with adoption as a later-stage 4th — defer adoption KRs until critique-survivability has held; Type 5: pre-registration + calibration + hit-rate, with decision-quality postmortem as an optional 4th — and never omit the pre-registration KR for Type 5; Type 6: activation/retention/conversion + quality-pair + telemetry-discipline — every value KR must have a paired quality KR, and telemetry must be in place before the cycle starts) and ask the user to confirm or reshape — do not start from a blank page when a playbook applies.**
109	- **For each KR, fill all four sub-fields:**
110	  - **baseline** — current value or state (if unknown, the first issue in the initiative is to measure it)
111	  - **target** — value or state we expect at the end
112	  - **window** — when this is judged ("by end of cycle", "across next 4 cycles", "within 30 days")
113	  - **source** — where the evidence will live (a file path, a Linear query, a log, a cached report)
114	  - And tag each KR `[committed]` (must hit 1.0 — operability, no-silent-failure, baseline tracking) or `[aspirational]` (0.6–0.7 is success — outcome KRs, behaviour change, forecast calibration). A mixed OKR with 1–2 committed + 1–2 aspirational is the common shape.
115	- **Which repos does this touch?** Name them. Cross-repo scope is allowed; name it explicitly.
116	
117	**4. Probe — scope and kill condition.**
118	Two separate questions:
119	
120	- **Appetite — how many issues?** Guide: 5 ≈ small (1–2 days), 10 ≈ medium (full cycle slot), 15 ≈ large (fills the whole cycle). If the answer exceeds 15, the initiative needs splitting — flag this now.
121	- **Kill condition — when do we stop?** Name the observable state that says "the bet didn't work, walk away." An initiative with KRs but no kill condition becomes a zombie. Phrasing: "If [KR] fails for [N] consecutive cycles" / "If we ship [X] and [baseline metric] doesn't move" / "If we learn [Y] in research".
122	
123	**5. Synthesise into initiative format.**
124	Draft the six fields (OKR-shaped — Goal is the Objective; Key results are 3 observable states with full sub-field discipline). Use the template in the next section.
125	
126	Each KR's state should be one of: a binary pass/fail ("X works on the common path with no manual intervention"), a fitness function firing ("the guard fails the run loudly when Y"), or a measurable delta ("token footprint drops vs baseline"). No "improve X" / "better Y" / "run N times" language. Every KR must have all four sub-fields (baseline / target / window / source) and a `[committed|aspirational]` tag.
127	
128	Present the draft. Do not create the Linear project yet.
129	
130	**6. [GATE] User confirms the draft.**
131	Ask explicitly: "Does this capture the initiative correctly? Any changes to the Objective, the Key results, kill condition, or project type before I create the project?" Do not proceed until confirmed. Fixing a wrong problem statement, vague KR, or missing kill condition here takes one minute; fixing it mid-cycle costs days.
132	
133	**6.5. [GATE] Verification rubric — 10 cross-cutting rules.**
134	Run the following checks against the confirmed draft. Each rule is a binary pass/fail. Any failure returns the draft to Step 5 for repair — the Linear project is NOT created until all 10 pass.
135	
136	| # | Rule | Pass condition |
137	|---|---|---|
138	| 1 | All six fields present | Goal / Key results / Affected repos / Appetite / Kill condition / Project type are all non-empty |
139	| 2 | Goal names who + outcome | Sentence contains an affected party and a desired outcome — no solution verb ("build", "add", "integrate", "ship") |
140	| 3 | Initiative name is a goal/problem | Name does not open with a solution verb ("Build X", "Add Y", "Integrate Z") |
141	| 4 | At least 3 KRs, no more than 5 | KR count ≥ 3 and ≤ 5 |
142	| 5 | Every KR is an observable state | No KR contains "improve", "better", "enhance", or arbitrary count thresholds without a correctness or quality dimension |
143	| 6 | Every KR has all four sub-fields | Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank or "TBD" |
144	| 7 | Every KR is tagged | Each KR carries exactly one of `[committed]` or `[aspirational]` |
145	| 8 | Kill condition is present | Non-empty; names an observable state, not a vague clause ("if it doesn't work") |
146	| 9 | Project type is canonical | One of: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6 — not free text |
147	| 10 | Appetite is in issues | Expressed as "~N issues" — not days, weeks, or sprints |
148	
149	Report each failing rule by number with the specific text that triggered the failure. When all 10 pass, proceed to Step 7.
150	
151	**7. Create the Linear project.**
152	Call `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_project` with:
153	- `name`: goal or problem label — not a solution name, not a repo name
154	- `description`: the six-field initiative format (see template below)
155	- Status: Planned
156	
157	Confirm the project URL and share it.
158	
159	**8. Optional: assign known issues.**
160	If the user listed existing issues for this initiative, list them and offer to reassign them to the new project via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue`. Assign only the ones the user confirms.
161	
162	## Initiative description template
163	
164	```markdown
165	**Goal:** For [who], we want to [solve problem / achieve outcome].
166	
167	**Key results:**
168	
169	**KR1 [committed|aspirational]** — [observable state — binary pass/fail, fitness function firing, or measurable delta]
170	- baseline: [current value/state]
171	- target:   [target value/state]
172	- window:   [time frame]
173	- source:   [where the evidence will live — file path, Linear query, log, cached report]
174	
175	**KR2 [committed|aspirational]** — [observable state]
176	- baseline: ...
177	- target:   ...
178	- window:   ...
179	- source:   ...
180	
181	**KR3 [committed|aspirational]** — [observable state]
182	- baseline: ...
183	- target:   ...
184	- window:   ...
185	- source:   ...
186	
187	(3 KRs default; cap at 5. When the key results hold — or are definitively ruled out — the initiative is Done.)
188	
189	**Affected repos:** [list]
190	
191	**Appetite:** ~[N] issues
192	
193	**Kill condition:** [the observable state that says "stop pursuing this Objective"]
194	
195	**Project type:** [1: methodology | 2: personal product | 3: utility skill pack | 4: research/thesis | 5: equity research | 6: production]
196	```
197	
198	## Common rationalisations
199	
200	| Rationalisation | Rebuttal |
201	|---|---|
202	| "I know what the goal is — I don't need to write it down." | The KRs aren't for you right now; they're for the agent in the next session who has no memory of this conversation. Write them down. |
203	| "The key results will be obvious once the work is done." | Defining them after the work is done is how "shipped = done" creeps in. KRs are what convert a list of closed issues into an achieved outcome. |
204	| "One KR is enough — the goal sentence covers the rest." | One KR collapses easily into a single arbitrary threshold. 3 KRs force you to name the dimensions that actually matter (correctness, no-blocking, no-silent-failure, speed) — and that's the discipline. |
205	| "I don't know the baseline — I'll add it later." | If baseline isn't known, the first issue in the initiative is to measure it. A KR without a baseline is an aspiration, not a result — you can't grade it at cycle close. |
206	| "The target is obvious from the goal — I don't need to spell it out separately." | The target is what makes the KR scoreable. Without it, "we'll know it when we see it" replaces a binary pass/fail, and the post-launch review degrades to vibes. |
207	| "I don't need a window — we'll just keep at it until it lands." | Without a window, a KR cannot be closed. Open-ended KRs become zombies — they neither succeed nor fail, they just live on the project page forever. |
208	| "The source of evidence is obvious — it's just whether the feature works." | Name the artefact. A file path, a Linear query, a log line, a cached report. If you can't name where the evidence lives, the KR isn't inspectable — and an uninspectable KR is a feeling, not a result. |
209	| "Why a kill condition? We're going to make this work." | Most bets don't pay off on the first try. The kill condition is what protects the next quarter from a sunk-cost zombie. Naming it up front costs one sentence; not naming it costs an entire cycle. |
```

Perfect. Now I have everything I need. Let me compile a comprehensive report:

---

## Brief: The case for the 3 KR minimum in initiative-shape

Based on a thorough search of /Users/anton/src/pde-skills, here's what the repo explains about why the ≥3 rule exists and what relaxing it would risk:

### What problem was the ≥3 rule designed to prevent?

The core problem is **collapsing into a single arbitrary threshold**. Per the rationalisation table (SKILL.md:204):

> "One KR collapses easily into a single arbitrary threshold. **3 KRs force you to name the dimensions that actually matter (correctness, no-blocking, no-silent-failure, speed) — and that's the discipline.**"

The rule forces multi-dimensional thinking rather than letting teams declare victory on a single binary or metric. Two KRs is still too sparse to prevent this collapse: you end up with "outcome + one guard rail," which leaves the outcome underspecified along other dimensions.

### The theoretical basis: Wodtke's "weekly-trackable" test

The phrase "per Wodtke" at SKILL.md:108 references Wodtke's OKR standard for **weekly-trackable, measurable KRs**. The repo applies this standard in the anti-pattern at SKILL.md:212:

> "'Improve quality' fails Wodtke's weekly-trackable test. Reshape into a decision-quality KR: in ≥X of last N sampled invocations, [the outcome] surfaced an issue... Now it has a sample, target, and window — and can be graded at cycle close."

Wodtke's insight isn't specifically "use 3 KRs," but rather that KRs must be measurable and traceable to observable states. The "3 KRs default" (capped at 5) emerged empirically as the sweet spot: enough to name competing dimensions, not so many that the initiative becomes a laundry list.

### The empirical basis: Six-type taxonomy

The repo discovered that each of the six initiative types needs a **specific minimum KR set to be gradable**:

- **Type 1 (methodology)**: requires invocation-rate (leading) + decision-quality (lagging) + anti-output guard → 3 dimensions
- **Type 2 (personal product)**: requires correctness (lagging) + no-silent-failure (operability) + maintenance-burden (operability) → 3 dimensions
- **Type 3 (utility skill pack)**: requires first-shot correctness (lagging) + coverage (committed) + use-log discipline (committed) → 3 dimensions
- **Type 4 (research)**: requires knowledge-claim (committed) + critique-survivability (lagging) + source-discipline (committed) → 3 dimensions
- **Type 5 (equity)**: requires pre-registration (committed) + calibration (lagging) + hit-rate (lagging) → 3 dimensions
- **Type 6 (production)**: requires activation/retention/conversion (lagging) + quality KR (committed) + telemetry-discipline (committed) → 3 dimensions

Each type's "3 KRs" are load-bearing: drop one and the initiative becomes ungradable post-hoc.

### Are there counter-examples where 2 KRs are clearly better?

**No documented counter-examples exist in the repo.** The docs do not acknowledge scenarios where 2 KRs produce a *better* outcome than 3. The closest is the explicit permission to cap at 5, but no permission or rationale for going *below* 3. The red flag at SKILL.md:233 states:

> "Only 1 or 2 KRs — the initiative is probably under-specified along the dimensions that actually matter (default 3; cap 5)."

### Honest answer to "what do we lose by relaxing to 2?"

You lose **multi-dimensional grading**. With 2 KRs, you can measure "did we hit the outcome" (KR1) + "did we avoid the obvious failure mode" (KR2), but you cannot simultaneously grade on outcome + quality + operability (Type 2), or outcome + coverage + discipline (Type 3). The post-cycle review collapses into "did we hit the main KR, and was there a fire?" rather than a structured verdict across the named dimensions. This is the Wodtke problem: the moment you drop the third dimension, one of the competing concerns (speed, correctness, coverage, operability) becomes invisible at cycle close.

The commit that formalized this (ABA-170, e5e80b2) did not explain "why 3 instead of 2," only that the type-specific playbooks all require 3 as the minimum load-bearing set. If you relax to 2, you'd need to justify which dimension you're dropping from each type, and the verification rubric would need re-engineering for each type to remain gradable post-hoc.
```

> AGENT

Here's the honest answer — the rationale for ≥3 isn't a hard Wodtke rule; it's a discipline mechanism, and your filler-KR pain is real.

**Why ≥3 was chosen:**

1. **Anti-collapse argument** (the load-bearing one). From `SKILL.md:204` rationalisation table: *"One KR collapses easily into a single arbitrary threshold. 3 KRs force you to name the dimensions that actually matter (correctness, no-blocking, no-silent-failure, speed) — and that's the discipline."* The fear is that fewer KRs = the initiative gets graded on "did the headline number move" without naming the competing concerns (quality, operability, safety).

2. **Type-playbook empirical basis.** Each of the 6 types defines a 3-KR "load-bearing minimum set" — e.g. Type 2 = correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden; Type 6 = activation/retention + quality-pair + telemetry-discipline. The "3" came from "the smallest number of dimensions you can drop and still grade a Type-N initiative honestly at cycle close." Drop to 2 and you're silently omitting one of those dimensions per type.

3. **Wodtke is NOT the source of "3."** `SKILL.md:108`'s "(Cap at 5; default 3 — per Wodtke.)" is misleading. Wodtke's actual standard is "weekly-trackable, measurable KRs" — not a specific count. The "3" is an empirical sweet spot the team chose, not a Wodtke commandment.

4. **There's an explicit Red flag at `SKILL.md:233`:** *"Only 1 or 2 KRs — the initiative is probably under-specified along the dimensions that actually matter."* So the skill currently treats 2 KRs as a known failure mode.

**What you lose at 2:** the guarantee that every initiative is graded across ≥3 dimensions. The post-cycle review degrades from "we hit outcome, quality, and discipline" to "we hit outcome and one guard rail."

**What you gain at 2:** an exit from the filler-KR trap you're hitting. The skill doesn't currently acknowledge that *bad* 3rd KRs are worse than no 3rd KR.

There's a hidden third option worth considering: the real problem you're describing isn't "≥3 is too strict" — it's "the rubric rewards quantity over quality." A KR that exists to satisfy rule #4 is exactly the kind of filler rule #5 ("Every KR is an observable state") should already catch but doesn't.

  "content": "# Plan — File ticket: tighten `/initiative-shape` KR-quality gate so filler-KRs are rejected\n\n*(Title pivot: the original task was \"loosen the ≥3 floor.\" After the crit review surfaced the question \"what do we lose at 2,\" and the user picked option C, this is now a ticket about **strengthening the quality rules** so padding to 3 with filler is no longer the path of least resistance. The floor stays at 3.)*\n\n## Context\n\nThe `/initiative-shape` skill enforces a Step 6.5 rubric. Rule #4 requires `KR count ≥ 3 and ≤ 5`. Rule #5 requires each KR to be an \"observable state\" and blocks language like \"improve\", \"better\", \"enhance\".\n\nIn real use, the user has been hitting a failure mode the current rubric doesn't catch: when the initiative only has 2 *load-bearing* dimensions, the user pads to 3 with a low-quality KR just to clear rule #4. The padded KR doesn't fail any current rubric check, so the gate passes — but the KR is filler and the post-cycle review degrades.\n\nThe honest fix is not to loosen the count (that would silently allow under-specified initiatives — see rationale section below). The fix is to make the **quality** gate strong enough that filler KRs are rejected at draft time, forcing the user to either find a real 3rd dimension or reshape the initiative scope.\n\n## Why the floor stays at 3 (decision recorded)\n\nThe ≥3 floor exists to force multi-dimensional grading. Each of the 6 type playbooks defines a 3-KR load-bearing minimum (e.g. Type 2 = correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden). Dropping below 3 silently omits one of those dimensions and degrades the post-cycle review from \"did outcome + quality + discipline hold?\" to \"did outcome + one guard rail hold?\"\n\nUser-observed counter-pressure: forced 3-KR drafts produce filler. That is a real pain — but the right response is to tighten the quality check, not the count. Filler is a symptom of an over-scoped initiative or a missing dimension; both are conversations the gate should provoke, not paper over.\n\n## What \"filler KR\" actually looks like (the patterns the new rules must catch)\n\nThese are real patterns the current rubric lets through:\n\n1. **Target = baseline.** \"users complete checkout — baseline 100%, target 100%, window: end of cycle, source: logs.\" Zero delta. Carries no information.\n2. **Two KRs measuring the same dimension.** KR1 \"test pass rate ≥95%\" + KR3 \"test coverage ≥90%\" both measure \"tests are good.\" Looks like 3 dimensions; is one.\n3. **KR is the Goal sentence in different words.** Goal: \"for users of /stock-screen, we want decision quality to improve.\" Filler KR: \"decision quality improves.\" It restates rather than names a measurable property.\n4. **Sub-field that points nowhere.** `source: the logs` / `source: the system` / `source: dashboard` / `baseline: currently unknown` / `target: better than today`. Currently passes rule #6 (\"none blank or TBD\") but doesn't point to a concrete artefact.\n5. **Meta-KRs added for gate compliance.** \"We will document this initiative\" / \"we will write tests\" / \"we will instrument logging\" — added to hit 3, not because they were load-bearing pre-gate.\n\n## Proposed rubric changes\n\n### Rule #5 — strengthen (current text + new sub-checks)\n\nCurrent pass condition: *\"No KR contains 'improve', 'better', 'enhance', or arbitrary count thresholds without a correctness or quality dimension.\"*\n\nReplace with a multi-part check (all must pass):\n\n- **5a. No degenerate KRs.** Every KR has a non-trivial target: `target ≠ baseline` either numerically (different number) or by state change (different observable state). Bans \"baseline: 100%, target: 100%\" patterns.\n- **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** Across all KRs, no two share the same dimension. Dimensions to consider: correctness, quality (perceived/measured), coverage, no-silent-failure, operability, adoption, calibration, discipline (artefact / process), maintenance-burden, performance. Pass condition: each KR maps to a distinct dimension name.\n- **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal sentence to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state should match it. Pass condition: every KR names a *measurable property of* the Goal — not the Goal itself.\n- **5d. Existing language ban.** Keep the current ban on \"improve\", \"better\", \"enhance\", and arbitrary count thresholds.\n\n### Rule #6 — strengthen (sub-field discipline)\n\nCurrent pass condition: *\"Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank or 'TBD'.\"*\n\nReplace with:\n\n- Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank, none equal to one of the placeholder phrases: `TBD`, `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`.\n- `source` must point to a concrete artefact: a file path, a directory, a Linear query, a log query (e.g. `bigquery: events WHERE ...`), a cached report URL, or a metric name + system (e.g. `prometheus: http_requests_total{}`).\n\n### Rationalisations table — new row\n\n| Rationalisation | Rebuttal |\n|---|---|\n| \"I need a 3rd KR — let me add one for documentation / tests / logging.\" | If your 3rd KR's only reason to exist is the gate, you have two real dimensions and you're padding. Padding with a meta-KR that wasn't load-bearing pre-gate is filler. Two honest paths: (a) **reshape** — the initiative is over-scoped, or a real third dimension (often: operability, calibration, or anti-output-guard) is hiding; (b) **descope** — narrow the initiative until 2 KRs are honestly sufficient and the work fits an ops slot rather than an initiative slot. Padding to clear the gate is the failure mode the gate exists to catch. |\n\n### Red flag (line ~233) — no change\n\nThe existing red flag *\"Only 1 or 2 KRs — the initiative is probably under-specified along the dimensions that actually matter (default 3; cap 5)\"* stays as-is. It still reflects the design intent.\n\n## Files to change (named for the ticket body — not edited in this plan)\n\n| File | Section | Change |\n|---|---|---|\n| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 rubric, rule #5 | Replace single-row rule with 5a/5b/5c/5d (preserve table format — either expand to 4 sub-rows or rephrase rule #5's pass condition to enumerate the four checks) |\n| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 rubric, rule #6 | Extend placeholder ban list and require concrete-artefact format for `source` |\n| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Common rationalisations table (~line 200–210) | Add the new row above |\n| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 prose around rubric | Reword failure-report instruction to handle the new sub-rules (e.g. \"Report each failing rule by number — for rule 5, name which sub-check (5a/5b/5c/5d) failed.\") |\n\nNo edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md` (the 3-5 count range stays correct). No edits to `references/initiative-types.md` (type playbooks unchanged). No edits to the template footer at line 187 (still \"3 KRs default; cap at 5\").\n\n## The ticket\n\n**Team:** Personal (only team in workspace)\n**Project:** Ops - bugs, maintenance, emergent (project ID `012923d7-25b5-475b-a1a7-5832a54ed389`)\n**Cycle:** none (backlog; pull into a future ops slot when ready)\n**Assignee:** none on create\n**Priority:** 4 (Low) — quality-of-life refinement, not blocking\n\n**Title:** `Tighten /initiative-shape KR-quality gate to reject filler KRs`\n\n**Description (markdown):**\n\n```markdown\n## Problem\n\nThe `/initiative-shape` Step 6.5 rubric requires ≥3 KRs (rule #4). In real use, this floor produces a failure mode the rubric doesn't catch: when an initiative only has 2 *load-bearing* dimensions, the user pads to 3 with a filler KR just to clear the gate. Currently passes rule #6 (\"none blank or TBD\") but doesn't point to a concrete artefact.\n5. **Meta-KRs added for gate compliance.** \"We will document this initiative\" / \"we will write tests\" / \"we will instrument logging\" — added to hit 3, not because they were load-bearing pre-gate.\n\n## Proposed rubric changes\n\n### Rule #5 — strengthen (current text + new sub-checks)\n\nCurrent pass condition: *\"No KR contains 'improve', 'better', 'enhance', or arbitrary count thresholds without a correctness or quality dimension.\"*\n\nReplace with a multi-part check (all must pass):\n\n- **5a. No degenerate KRs.** Every KR has a non-trivial target: `target ≠ baseline` either numerically (different number) or by state change (different observable state). Bans \"baseline: 100%, target: 100%\" patterns.\n- **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** Across all KRs, no two share the same dimension. Dimensions to consider: correctness, quality (perceived/measured), coverage, no-silent-failure, operability, adoption, calibration, discipline (artefact / process), maintenance-burden, performance. Pass condition: each KR maps to a distinct dimension name.\n- **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal sentence to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state should match it. Pass condition: every KR names a *measurable property of* the Goal — not the Goal itself.\n- **5d. Existing language ban.** Keep the current ban on \"improve\", \"better\", \"enhance\", and arbitrary count thresholds.\n\n### Rule #6 — strengthen (sub-field discipline)\n\nCurrent pass condition: *\"Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank or 'TBD'.\"*\n\nReplace with:\n\n- Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank, none equal to one of the placeholder phrases: `TBD`, `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`.\n- `source` must point to a concrete artefact: a file path, a directory, a Linear query, a log query (e.g. `bigquery: events WHERE ...`), a cached report URL, or a metric name + system (e.g. `prometheus: http_requests_total{}`).\n\n### Rationalisations table — new row\n\n| Rationalisation | Rebuttal |\n|---|---|\n| \"I need a 3rd KR — let me add one for documentation / tests / logging.\" | If your 3rd KR's only reason to exist is the gate, you have two real dimensions and you're padding. Padding with a meta-KR that wasn't load-bearing pre-gate is filler. Two honest paths: (a) **reshape** — the initiative is over-scoped, or a real third dimension (often: operability, calibration, or anti-output-guard) is hiding; (b) **descope** — narrow the initiative until 2 KRs are honestly sufficient and the work fits an ops slot rather than an initiative slot. Padding to clear the gate is the failure mode the gate exists to catch. |\n\n### Red flag (line ~233) — no change\n\nThe existing red flag *\"Only 1 or 2 KRs — the initiative is probably under-specified along the dimensions that actually matter (default 3; cap 5)\"* stays as-is. It still reflects the design intent.\n\n## Files to change (named for the ticket body — not edited in this plan)\n\n| File | Section | Change |\n|---|---|---|\n| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 rubric, rule #5 | Replace single-row rule with 5a/5b/5c/5d (preserve table format — either expand to 4 sub-rows or rephrase rule #5's pass condition to enumerate the four checks) |\n| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 rubric, rule #6 | Extend placeholder ban list and require concrete-artefact format for `source` |\n| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Common rationalisations table (~line 200–210) | Add the new row above |\n| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 prose around rubric | Reword failure-report instruction to handle the new sub-rules (e.g. \"Report each failing rule by number — for rule 5, name which sub-check (5a/5b/5c/5d) failed.\") |\n\nNo edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md` (the 3-5 count range stays correct). No edits to `references/initiative-types.md` (type playbooks unchanged). No edits to the template footer at line 187 (still \"3 KRs default; cap at 5\").\n\n## The ticket\n\n**Team:** Personal (only team in workspace)\n**Project:** Ops - bugs, maintenance, emergent (project ID `012923d7-25b5-475b-a1a7-5832a54ed389`)\n**Cycle:** none (backlog; pull into a future ops slot when ready)\n**Assignee:** none on create\n**Priority:** 4 (Low) — quality-of-life refinement, not blocking\n\n**Title:** `Tighten /initiative-shape KR-quality gate to reject filler KRs`\n\n**Description (markdown):**\n\n```markdown\n## Problem\n\nThe `/initiative-shape` Step 6.5 rubric requires ≥3 KRs (rule #4). In real use, this floor produces a failure mode the rubric doesn't catch: when an initiative only has 2 *load-bearing* dimensions, the user pads to 3 with a filler KR just to clear the gate. The padded KR passes rule #5 (\"observable state\") and rule #6 (\"sub-fields filled\") at face value, but the KR is meaningless and the post-cycle review degrades.\n\nThe ≥3 floor itself is load-bearing — each of the 6 type playbooks defines a 3-KR minimum that maps to grading dimensions (correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden, etc.). Loosening the count would silently allow under-specified initiatives. The right fix is to make the **quality** gate strong enough that filler is rejected at draft time, so the user is forced to either find a real 3rd dimension or descope the initiative.\n\n## Filler patterns the new rules must catch\n\n1. **Target = baseline.** \"baseline 100%, target 100%\" — zero delta, no information.\n2. **Two KRs measuring the same dimension.** KR1 \"test pass rate ≥95%\" + KR3 \"test coverage ≥90%\" — both = \"tests are good.\"\n3. **KR is the Goal restated.** Goal: \"decision quality improves.\" KR: \"decision quality improves.\"\n4. **Sub-field points nowhere.** `source: the logs` / `baseline: currently unknown` / `target: better than today`.\n5. **Meta-KRs added for gate compliance.** \"We will document\" / \"we will write tests\" added to hit the count, not load-bearing pre-gate.\n\n## Change — rubric rule #5\n\nReplace the current single-row rule with four sub-checks:\n\n* **5a. No degenerate KRs.** `target ≠ baseline` either numerically or by state change.\n* **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** No two KRs share the same dimension (correctness / quality / coverage / no-silent-failure / operability / adoption / calibration / discipline / maintenance-burden / performance).\n* **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it.\n* **5d. Existing language ban.** Keep the ban on \"improve\", \"better\", \"enhance\", and arbitrary count thresholds without a quality dimension.\n\n## Change — rubric rule #6\n\nExtend the placeholder ban list: also reject `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`. Require `source` to point to a concrete artefact: file path, directory, Linear query, log query, cached report URL, or metric name + system.\n\n## Change — rationalisations table\n\nAdd a row covering the \"I need a 3rd KR — let me add documentation / tests / logging\" pattern, with the rebuttal \"if the 3rd KR only exists to clear the gate, reshape or descope — don't pad.\"\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #5 enumerates 5a/5b/5c/5d with concrete pass conditions.\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #6 has the extended placeholder ban list and the concrete-artefact requirement for `source`.\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rationalisations table has the new \"padding to 3\" row.\n* Step 6.5 failure-report instruction names sub-rules (5a/5b/5c/5d) when failing.\n* Manual smoke test against each of the 5 filler patterns: re-run `/initiative-shape` with a draft that exhibits the pattern; confirm the gate rejects it with a pointer to the specific sub-rule.\n* Manual smoke test against a legitimate 3-KR draft: confirm it still passes.\n\n## Files\n\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` — rubric rules #5 and #6 in Step 6.5, plus the rationalisations table around lines 200-210.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n* Changing rule #4 (count range stays `≥ 3 and ≤ 5`).\n* Changing the default KR count (still 3).\n* Changing type playbooks in `references/initiative-types.md`.\n* Edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md`.\n* Adding a \"waive the floor with justification\" override path.\n\n## Why this is small\n\nThree sub-edits to one file. No new behaviour; the changes make existing intent (multi-dimensional, observable, evidence-pointing KRs) actually enforceable. The dimension-name list in 5b is the only piece that needs careful authoring — the rest is text changes.\n```\n\n## Execution after ExitPlanMode\n\nOne MCP call:\n\n```\nmcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue(\n  team=\"Personal\",\n  project=\"Ops - bugs, maintenance, emergent\",\n  title=\"Tighten /initiative-shape KR-quality gate to reject filler KRs\",\n  description=<body above>,\n  priority=4,\n)\n```\n\nThen report the issue URL.\n\n## Verification\n\n* The MCP call returns an issue URL on the Personal team in the Ops container project.\n* Opening the URL shows the ticket with title and body matching the spec and no cycle assigned.\n* No other Linear changes.\n",

## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# Plan — File ticket: tighten `/initiative-shape` KR-quality gate so filler-KRs are rejected

*(Title pivot: the original task was "loosen the ≥3 floor." After the crit review surfaced the question "what do we lose at 2," and the user picked option C, this is now a ticket about **strengthening the quality rules** so padding to 3 with filler is no longer the path of least resistance. The floor stays at 3.)*

## Context

The `/initiative-shape` skill enforces a Step 6.5 rubric. Rule #4 requires `KR count ≥ 3 and ≤ 5`. Rule #5 requires each KR to be an "observable state" and blocks language like "improve", "better", "enhance".

In real use, the user has been hitting a failure mode the current rubric doesn't catch: when the initiative only has 2 *load-bearing* dimensions, the user pads to 3 with a low-quality KR just to clear rule #4. The padded KR doesn't fail any current rubric check, so the gate passes — but the KR is filler and the post-cycle review degrades.

The honest fix is not to loosen the count (that would silently allow under-specified initiatives — see rationale section below). The fix is to make the **quality** gate strong enough that filler KRs are rejected at draft time, forcing the user to either find a real 3rd dimension or reshape the initiative scope.

## Why the floor stays at 3 (decision recorded)

The ≥3 floor exists to force multi-dimensional grading. Each of the 6 type playbooks defines a 3-KR load-bearing minimum (e.g. Type 2 = correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden). Dropping below 3 silently omits one of those dimensions and degrades the post-cycle review from "did outcome + quality + discipline hold?" to "did outcome + one guard rail hold?"

User-observed counter-pressure: forced 3-KR drafts produce filler. That is a real pain — but the right response is to tighten the quality check, not the count. Filler is a symptom of an over-scoped initiative or a missing dimension; both are conversations the gate should provoke, not paper over.

## What "filler KR" actually looks like (the patterns the new rules must catch)

These are real patterns the current rubric lets through:

1. **Target = baseline.** "users complete checkout — baseline 100%, target 100%, window: end of cycle, source: logs." Zero delta. Carries no information.
2. **Two KRs measuring the same dimension.** KR1 "test pass rate ≥95%" + KR3 "test coverage ≥90%" both measure "tests are good." Looks like 3 dimensions; is one.
3. **KR is the Goal sentence in different words.** Goal: "for users of /stock-screen, we want decision quality to improve." Filler KR: "decision quality improves." It restates rather than names a measurable property.
4. **Sub-field that points nowhere.** `source: the logs` / `source: the system` / `source: dashboard` / `baseline: currently unknown` / `target: better than today`. Currently passes rule #6 ("none blank or TBD") but doesn't point to a concrete artefact.
5. **Meta-KRs added for gate compliance.** "We will document this initiative" / "we will write tests" / "we will instrument logging" — added to hit 3, not because they were load-bearing pre-gate.

## Proposed rubric changes

### Rule #5 — strengthen (current text + new sub-checks)

Current pass condition: *"No KR contains 'improve', 'better', 'enhance', or arbitrary count thresholds without a correctness or quality dimension."*

Replace with a multi-part check (all must pass):

- **5a. No degenerate KRs.** Every KR has a non-trivial target: `target ≠ baseline` either numerically (different number) or by state change (different observable state). Bans "baseline: 100%, target: 100%" patterns.
- **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** Across all KRs, no two share the same dimension. Dimensions to consider: correctness, quality (perceived/measured), coverage, no-silent-failure, operability, adoption, calibration, discipline (artefact / process), maintenance-burden, performance. Pass condition: each KR maps to a distinct dimension name.
- **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal sentence to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state should match it. Pass condition: every KR names a *measurable property of* the Goal — not the Goal itself.
- **5d. Existing language ban.** Keep the current ban on "improve", "better", "enhance", and arbitrary count thresholds.

### Rule #6 — strengthen (sub-field discipline)

Current pass condition: *"Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank or 'TBD'."*

Replace with:

- Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank, none equal to one of the placeholder phrases: `TBD`, `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`.
- `source` must point to a concrete artefact: a file path, a directory, a Linear query, a log query (e.g. `bigquery: events WHERE ...`), a cached report URL, or a metric name + system (e.g. `prometheus: http_requests_total{}`).

### Rationalisations table — new row

| Rationalisation | Rebuttal |
|---|---|
| "I need a 3rd KR — let me add one for documentation / tests / logging." | If your 3rd KR's only reason to exist is the gate, you have two real dimensions and you're padding. Padding with a meta-KR that wasn't load-bearing pre-gate is filler. Two honest paths: (a) **reshape** — the initiative is over-scoped, or a real third dimension (often: operability, calibration, or anti-output-guard) is hiding; (b) **descope** — narrow the initiative until 2 KRs are honestly sufficient and the work fits an ops slot rather than an initiative slot. Padding to clear the gate is the failure mode the gate exists to catch. |

### Red flag (line ~233) — no change

The existing red flag *"Only 1 or 2 KRs — the initiative is probably under-specified along the dimensions that actually matter (default 3; cap 5)"* stays as-is. It still reflects the design intent.

## Files to change (named for the ticket body — not edited in this plan)

| File | Section | Change |
|---|---|---|
| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 rubric, rule #5 | Replace single-row rule with 5a/5b/5c/5d (preserve table format — either expand to 4 sub-rows or rephrase rule #5's pass condition to enumerate the four checks) |
| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 rubric, rule #6 | Extend placeholder ban list and require concrete-artefact format for `source` |
| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Common rationalisations table (~line 200–210) | Add the new row above |
| `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` | Step 6.5 prose around rubric | Reword failure-report instruction to handle the new sub-rules (e.g. "Report each failing rule by number — for rule 5, name which sub-check (5a/5b/5c/5d) failed.") |

No edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md` (the 3-5 count range stays correct). No edits to `references/initiative-types.md` (type playbooks unchanged). No edits to the template footer at line 187 (still "3 KRs default; cap at 5").

**Title:** `Tighten /initiative-shape KR-quality gate to reject filler KRs`

**Description (markdown):**

```markdown
## Problem

The `/initiative-shape` Step 6.5 rubric requires ≥3 KRs (rule #4). In real use, this floor produces a failure mode the rubric doesn't catch: when an initiative only has 2 *load-bearing* dimensions, the user pads to 3 with a filler KR just to clear the gate. The padded KR passes rule #5 ("observable state") and rule #6 ("sub-fields filled") at face value, but the KR is meaningless and the post-cycle review degrades.

The ≥3 floor itself is load-bearing — each of the 6 type playbooks defines a 3-KR minimum that maps to grading dimensions (correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden, etc.). Loosening the count would silently allow under-specified initiatives. The right fix is to make the **quality** gate strong enough that filler is rejected at draft time, so the user is forced to either find a real 3rd dimension or descope the initiative.

## Filler patterns the new rules must catch

1. **Target = baseline.** "baseline 100%, target 100%" — zero delta, no information.
2. **Two KRs measuring the same dimension.** KR1 "test pass rate ≥95%" + KR3 "test coverage ≥90%" — both = "tests are good."
3. **KR is the Goal restated.** Goal: "decision quality improves." KR: "decision quality improves."
4. **Sub-field points nowhere.** `source: the logs` / `baseline: currently unknown` / `target: better than today`.
5. **Meta-KRs added for gate compliance.** "We will document" / "we will write tests" added to hit the count, not load-bearing pre-gate.

## Change — rubric rule #5

Replace the current single-row rule with four sub-checks:

* **5a. No degenerate KRs.** `target ≠ baseline` either numerically or by state change.
* **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** No two KRs share the same dimension (correctness / quality / coverage / no-silent-failure / operability / adoption / calibration / discipline / maintenance-burden / performance).
* **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it.
* **5d. Existing language ban.** Keep the ban on "improve", "better", "enhance", and arbitrary count thresholds without a quality dimension.

## Change — rubric rule #6

Extend the placeholder ban list: also reject `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`. Require `source` to point to a concrete artefact: file path, directory, Linear query, log query, cached report URL, or metric name + system.

## Change — rationalisations table

Add a row covering the "I need a 3rd KR — let me add documentation / tests / logging" pattern, with the rebuttal "if the 3rd KR only exists to clear the gate, reshape or descope — don't pad."

## Acceptance criteria

* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #5 enumerates 5a/5b/5c/5d with concrete pass conditions.
* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #6 has the extended placeholder ban list and the concrete-artefact requirement for `source`.
* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rationalisations table has the new "padding to 3" row.
* Step 6.5 failure-report instruction names sub-rules (5a/5b/5c/5d) when failing.
* Manual smoke test against each of the 5 filler patterns: re-run `/initiative-shape` with a draft that exhibits the pattern; confirm the gate rejects it with a pointer to the specific sub-rule.
* Manual smoke test against a legitimate 3-KR draft: confirm it still passes.

## Files

* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` — rubric rules #5 and #6 in Step 6.5, plus the rationalisations table around lines 200-210.

## Out of scope

* Changing rule #4 (count range stays `≥ 3 and ≤ 5`).
* Changing the default KR count (still 3).
* Changing type playbooks in `references/initiative-types.md`.
* Edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md`.
* Adding a "waive the floor with justification" override path.

## Why this is small

Three sub-edits to one file. No new behaviour; the changes make existing intent (multi-dimensional, observable, evidence-pointing KRs) actually enforceable. The dimension-name list in 5b is the only piece that needs careful authoring — the rest is text changes.
```

> DEVELOPER

in ticket aba-191 i can see these dimensions being listed correctness / quality / coverage / no-silent-failure / operability / adoption / calibration / discipline / maintenance-burden / performance

how many of these are distinct categories?

i want to calibrate the skill better against certain best practices
for example, when ever there's a KR written against something that can be considered a service level objective - there are some good practices on how to craft really good SLOs - those that measure availability, latency, correctness or completeness (as per the Google SRE book)

> AGENT

Honest count: **5–6 truly distinct categories**, with the rest being sub-dimensions or vague labels.

## Collapsing the list

| Listed | Verdict | Folds into |
|---|---|---|
| **correctness** | distinct | — |
| **quality** | not distinct — too vague | depends on what it meant: usually correctness, calibration, or SRE's "degraded response quality" |
| **coverage** | not distinct as written | sub-dim of correctness (test/spec coverage) or of SRE freshness/coverage for data systems |
| **no-silent-failure** | not distinct | sub-dim of operability/observability |
| **operability** | distinct | — |
| **adoption** | distinct | — |
| **calibration** | not distinct | sub-dim of correctness for probabilistic systems |
| **discipline** | distinct (meta-category — about the *process*, not the system) | — |
| **maintenance-burden** | distinct | — |
| **performance** | distinct | — |

So the real taxonomy is roughly: **correctness, performance, operability, adoption, maintenance-burden, discipline.**

## Where SRE's SLO taxonomy fits

The SRE book splits "is the runtime system meeting its bar?" into a per-system-type breakdown:

| System type | SLIs |
|---|---|
| User-facing serving | availability, latency, throughput |
| Storage | latency, availability, durability |
| Data / batch pipelines | throughput, end-to-end latency, coverage, freshness, correctness |
| All systems | correctness |

That doesn't replace the 6 above — it **refines** what counts as a good KR inside "correctness" / "performance" / "operability" when the initiative is shipping a runtime service. SRE answers "what's a high-quality KR for an SLO," not "what dimensions does an initiative span."

## Suggested calibration move

Two-layer dimension model instead of one flat list:

**Layer 1 — initiative-level dimensions** (what rule 5b checks for distinctness across KRs):
correctness · performance · operability · adoption · maintenance-burden · discipline

**Layer 2 — SLO-shaped KR template** (used when a KR is measuring a runtime service, regardless of which Layer 1 dimension it sits in):
- pick an SLI category appropriate to the system type (availability / latency / throughput / durability / freshness / correctness)
- name the SLI as a ratio (good events / valid events) per SRE
- set a target window (e.g. 99.9% over rolling 30 days)
- name the error budget

This way Layer 1 enforces "different dimensions across KRs" and Layer 2 enforces "any SLO-shaped KR is *actually* SLO-shaped" — they don't fight.

Want me to:
1. update ABA-191 to drop the bad sub-categories and reference the SRE layering, or
2. open a separate follow-up ticket for the SLO-template work (Layer 2), keeping ABA-191 scoped to Layer 1?

> DEVELOPER

that looks good, but i want to make sure we have a layer 2 for other categories too

> AGENT

Good push. Here's the Layer 2 catalogue across all six Layer 1 dimensions, with the known-good source for each so we're not synthesising where we don't need to.

## Layer 2 per Layer 1 dimension

| Layer 1 dimension | Layer 2 template family | What a good KR includes | Canonical source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Correctness** | SRE SLI/SLO (event-ratio form) | numerator (good events) / denominator (valid events) + target % + rolling window + error budget. For probabilistic outputs: precision/recall, Brier score, calibration curve. | *Site Reliability Engineering*, Beyer et al., chs. 4, 6 |
| **Performance** | SRE SLO — latency + throughput | percentile (p50/p95/p99) + target ms + rolling window; OR ops/sec at saturation %; PLUS the "four golden signals" (latency, traffic, errors, saturation). | *SRE*, ch. 6 ("Monitoring Distributed Systems") |
| **Operability** | SRE incident-response + four golden signals | MTTD, MTTR, alert noise (signal:noise), runbook coverage, % of failure modes with named alert. KR shape: "for failure mode X, alert fires within N min, runbook step Y exists." | *SRE*, chs. 7, 11–14 |
| **Adoption** | Product-funnel (AARRR / pirate metrics) + cohort retention | funnel step (exposed → tried → activated → retained) + cohort (who) + window (when judged) + numerator/denominator over that cohort. Avoid raw counts without a denominator. | McClure, "Startup Metrics for Pirates"; Balfour/Reforge growth loops; Amplitude's "North Star" framework |
| **Maintenance-burden** | DORA four keys + structural delta | one of: deployment frequency, lead time for changes, change failure rate, MTTR. PLUS structural delta on the affected module (lines, cyclomatic complexity, public API surface, test coverage). KR shape: "in module X, change failure rate ≤ 5% over next 30 days; structural metric Y stays ≤ baseline." | DORA *Accelerate State of DevOps* reports; Forsgren/Humble/Kim *Accelerate* |
| **Discipline / process** | Artefact existence + freshness | named artefact (ADR-N, postmortem doc, design doc) + state (Accepted / Signed off) + supersedes link / review attendees + freshness window. KR shape: "ADR-N exists, status = Accepted by date X, supersedes ADR-M." | Nygard on ADRs; Allspaw on blameless postmortems; Larson on writing decisions down. (Weakest published taxonomy of the six — our own `eng-principles-universal.md` Rule A3/D1 is the in-repo canonical source.) |

## Honesty about strength

- **Strong, canonical, off-the-shelf:** correctness, performance, operability (SRE book is the reference).
- **Strong but multi-source:** adoption (no single canonical text — AARRR + Reforge + Amplitude triangulate it).
- **Strong on the metric side, weaker on KR-templating:** maintenance-burden (DORA is canonical for the four keys; structural-delta KRs are well-understood but no single template authority).
- **Weakest published source — our own rules are canonical:** discipline.

If you're OK with that, two follow-up decisions:

1. **Where does Layer 2 live?**
   - (a) Inline in the skill rubric — keeps everything in one file but pushes skill toward the 350-line cap.
   - (b) New reference file `references/kr-quality-templates.md`, cited from rule 5 — matches how type playbooks live in `references/initiative-types.md`. **Recommended.**

2. **How does this land in Linear?**
   - (a) Extend ABA-191 to cover Layer 2 (one bigger ticket).
   - (b) Keep ABA-191 scoped to Layer 1 (filler-KR rejection) and open a sibling ticket "Add SLI/DORA/funnel-based KR templates per dimension" so the two land independently and can be sequenced.

Which split do you want?

> DEVELOPER

just remember all the projects i have are personal ones, with no user facing traffic
so many of the frameworks aren't applicable

i just need to make sure that good goals that helps me prioritize the work affectively and that i have good tools to measure if things i'm building are working as intended - thats more important

> AGENT

Good cut. That collapses the taxonomy hard. With no production traffic, no real users to funnel, and no on-call, most of the SRE/DORA/adoption machinery is dead weight. The actual question for your projects is: *did I build the right thing, and is it working for me?*

## Re-grounded Layer 1 (personal projects)

Four dimensions, not six. Three are usually load-bearing per initiative; the fourth fills a gap when it's there.

| Dimension | What it answers | When it's load-bearing |
|---|---|---|
| **Correctness** | does the thing produce the intended output on the cases I care about | always, for any build |
| **Outcome / behaviour change** | does the thing I built actually change what I do or how I work | most product-shaped initiatives (Types 2, 4, 5, 6 from `references/initiative-types.md`) |
| **Maintenance / future-me cost** | can I come back in 30 days and still navigate this | always — solo means future-you pays the whole tax |
| **Discipline / completion** | did the artefacts get finished, or did this half-ship | always — solo means nobody else will finish it |

Performance, operability-as-SLO, adoption-as-funnel: dropped. They re-enter only if a specific initiative actually has runtime traffic (e.g. a deployed service) — that becomes a per-initiative exception, not the default vocabulary.

## Layer 2 — measurement templates that work without instrumentation

| Layer 1 | Template | What a good KR includes | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Correctness** | Golden-path test | named input(s) → named expected output → how it's verified (script run, snapshot diff, eyeball check) → judged-by date | "Run `/initiative-shape` against 5 fixture inputs in `fixtures/`; all 5 produce the 6-field structure verified by a checked-in snapshot diff; judged at cycle close" |
| **Outcome** | Self-trial protocol | the next N times you hit situation X, observable behaviour Y happens (or doesn't); judged by inspecting the resulting artefacts | "Next 5 initiatives shaped after this lands: zero filler KRs, judged by reading the Linear project descriptions" |
| **Maintenance** | Structural cap + revisit gate | size/shape cap (LOC, section count, file count) stays within a stated bound; revisit-at-N-days check that you can still navigate without re-reading | "`SKILL.md` stays ≤ 300 lines; in 30 days I can answer 'where does rule 5c live' from memory" |
| **Discipline** | Artefact-exists + sections-complete | named artefact exists, named sections populated, no TBD/placeholder, predecessor/supersedes declared if applicable | "ABA-191 closes only after rule 5 has 5a/5b/5c/5d each with a 1-line pass condition, zero TBDs, and the rationalisations row added" |

Every template here is something you can verify by yourself, on your laptop, without dashboards or telemetry. That's the constraint.

## What this changes about ABA-191

Three small adjustments:

1. **Rule 5b dimension list** shrinks to the four above (correctness / outcome / maintenance / discipline) — dropping performance, operability, adoption, calibration, coverage, no-silent-failure as defaults. They become "available only when the initiative genuinely has them."
2. **A new short reference file** (`references/kr-quality-templates.md`) holds the four Layer 2 templates above, cited from rule 5/6.
3. **No SRE/DORA/AARRR references** — they'd be misapplied at this scale.

This also touches the "Goal" side of your prioritization comment: the four dimensions above are exactly what a goal needs to commit to in order to be prioritizable — *what's the right answer (correctness), what's the behavior change (outcome), what's the future-me tax (maintenance), what's the completion bar (discipline)*. Anything that can't answer all four cleanly is probably not worth a cycle slot.

Want me to:
- (a) update ABA-191 in place — replace the dimension list and add a sub-task for the reference file, or
- (b) open a sibling ticket for the reference file + dimension-list rewrite, keeping ABA-191 narrow on the rubric edits?

{"id":"ABA-191","title":"Tighten /initiative-shape KR-quality gate to reject filler KRs","description":"## Problem\n\nThe `/initiative-shape` Step 6.5 rubric requires ≥3 KRs (rule #4). The padded KR passes rule #5 (\"observable state\") and rule #6 (\"sub-fields filled\") at face value, but the KR is meaningless and the post-cycle review degrades.\n\nThe ≥3 floor itself is load-bearing — each of the 6 type playbooks defines a 3-KR minimum that maps to grading dimensions (correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden, etc.). Loosening the count would silently allow under-specified initiatives. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it.\n* **5d. They were enterprise-shaped sub-dimensions; they re-enter only as per-initiative exceptions when a specific initiative genuinely ships runtime traffic.\n\n## Layer 2 — measurement templates (one per Layer 1 dimension)\n\nLives in a new reference file `references/kr-quality-templates.md`, cited from rule 5 in the skill.\n\n| Layer 1 | Template | What a good KR includes | Example |\n| -- | -- | -- | -- |\n| **Correctness** | Golden-path test | named input(s) → named expected output → how it's verified (script run, snapshot diff, eyeball check) → judged-by date | \"Run `/initiative-shape` against 5 fixture inputs in `fixtures/`; all 5 produce the 6-field structure verified by a checked-in snapshot diff; judged at cycle close\" |\n| **Outcome** | Self-trial protocol | the next N times I hit situation X, observable behaviour Y happens (or doesn't); judged by inspecting the resulting artefacts | \"Next 5 initiatives shaped after this lands: zero filler KRs, judged by reading the Linear project descriptions\" |\n| **Maintenance** | Structural cap + revisit gate | size/shape cap (LOC, section count, file count) stays within a stated bound; revisit-at-N-days check that I can still navigate without re-reading | \"`SKILL.md` stays ≤ 300 lines; in 30 days I can answer 'where does rule 5c live' from memory\" |\n| **Discipline** | Artefact-exists + sections-complete | named artefact exists, named sections populated, no TBD/placeholder, predecessor/supersedes declared if applicable | \"<issue id=\"0b00aafe-b668-47db-8768-31eca82ad6f4\">ABA-191</issue> closes only after rule 5 has 5a/5b/5c/5d each with a 1-line pass condition, zero TBDs, and the rationalisations row added\" |\n\nEvery template above is verifiable by one person, locally, without instrumentation. That's the constraint.\n\n## Change — rubric rule #5\n\nReplace the current single-row rule with four sub-checks:\n\n* **5a. No degenerate KRs.** `target ≠ baseline` either numerically or by state change.\n* **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** No two KRs share the same Layer 1 dimension. Default vocabulary: correctness / outcome / maintenance / discipline (see `references/kr-quality-templates.md`). A KR may invoke an out-of-default dimension (e.g. performance) only when the initiative genuinely has that property.\n* **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it.\n* **5d. KR matches its Layer 2 template.** For each KR, name which Layer 1 dimension it sits in and confirm it matches the template in `references/kr-quality-templates.md`.\n* **5e. Existing language ban.** Keep the ban on \"improve\", \"better\", \"enhance\", and arbitrary count thresholds without a quality dimension.\n\n## Change — rubric rule #6\n\nExtend the placeholder ban list: also reject `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`. Require `source` to point to a concrete artefact: file path, directory, Linear query, log query, cached report URL, or metric name + system.\n\n## Change — rationalisations table\n\nAdd a row covering the \"I need a 3rd KR — let me add documentation / tests / logging\" pattern, with the rebuttal \"if the 3rd KR only exists to clear the gate, reshape or descope — don't pad.\"\n\n## New reference file — `references/kr-quality-templates.md`\n\nSingle new file. Contains:\n\n* The 4 Layer 1 dimensions with one-line definitions.\n* The 4 Layer 2 templates above, each with: when it applies, what fields it requires, one good example, one bad example.\n* A short \"out of scope for personal projects\" note naming the dimensions we deliberately don't use as defaults (performance, operability-as-SLO, adoption-as-funnel).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #5 enumerates 5a/5b/5c/5d/5e with concrete pass conditions.\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #6 has the extended placeholder ban list and the concrete-artefact requirement for `source`.\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rationalisations table has the new \"padding to 3\" row.\n* Step 6.5 failure-report instruction names sub-rules (5a/5b/5c/5d/5e) when failing.\n* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` exists with the 4 Layer 1 dimensions + 4 Layer 2 templates + out-of-scope note.\n* Skill rule 5b and 5d cite `references/kr-quality-templates.md`.\n* Manual smoke test against each of the 5 filler patterns: re-run `/initiative-shape` with a draft that exhibits the pattern; confirm the gate rejects it with a pointer to the specific sub-rule.\n* Manual smoke test against a legitimate 3-KR draft (across all 4 Layer 1 dimensions): confirm it still passes.\n\n## Files\n\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` — rubric rules #5 and #6 in Step 6.5, plus the rationalisations table around lines 200-210.\n* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` — new file.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n* Changing rule #4 (count range stays `≥ 3 and ≤ 5`).\n* Changing the default KR count (still 3).\n* Changing type playbooks in `references/initiative-types.md`.\n* Edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md`.\n* Adding a \"waive the floor with justification\" override path.\n* Importing SRE SLO / DORA / AARRR vocabulary as defaults. They can be cited for a single initiative that genuinely needs them, but they are not the default Layer 1 vocabulary.\n\n## Background\n\nOriginally filed after the user hit filler-KR pain on recent projects (padding to 3 KRs just to clear the gate). Initial direction was to loosen the floor from ≥3 to ≥2; after reviewing the rationale for the ≥3 floor (multi-dimensional grading; type playbooks all define 3-KR load-bearing minimums), the direction pivoted to strengthening the quality check instead. Floor stays at 3.\n\nA follow-up review (same session) collapsed the 10-dimension brainstorm to the 4 personal-project dimensions above, dropping SRE/DORA/AARRR machinery as misapplied at this scale. Layer 2 templates were added so each Layer 1 dimension has a known-good measurement form verifiable without instrumentation.","priority":{"value":4,"name":"Low"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-191/tighten-initiative-shape-kr-quality-gate-to-reject-filler-krs","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-191-tighten-initiative-shape-kr-quality-gate-to-reject-filler","createdAt":"2026-05-21T12:39:25.884Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-21T12:56:30.811Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Backlog","statusType":"backlog","labels":[],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Initiative quality - type-aware OKRs with KRs","projectId":"172c8fce-10cf-45a1-bc7c-ddd4d05083aa","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1"}

> DEVELOPER

i want to show you something that we did recently that worked well
as part of the user stories that were being created for a project
these two were created:

As the operator, I want each drain run to write a structured log (issue order, spawn timestamps, exit codes, final Linear state) to ~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>.json — so KR1 (completion %) is gradable from the file alone, and KR2’s time_spent block has a known home to be appended to at cycle close.

As the operator, I want to run a command that reads the run logs across recent cycles and prints a grade — per-cycle completion %, trend across the last N cycles, recurrent failure-mode tuples, and a verdict against the kill condition (OK / WATCH / KILL) — so I can decide whether to ship-as-is, iterate, or walk away, without re-reading JSON by hand.


--

i liked these because the outcomes were gradeable using automation, and we could even plan to build the automation

> AGENT

Yes — that's a strong pattern and it deserves to be a first-class part of the framework. Let me name it and extract the shape.

## The pattern — "grader-backed KR"

A KR is **grader-backed** when its `source:` points at a tool (script, command, query) that reads the write-side artefacts and emits a verdict — not at the raw artefacts themselves.

Two-part shape:

| Part | What it is | In your example |
|---|---|---|
| **Write-side artefact** | Every relevant event/run/state-change emits a structured record at a known location with a known schema. | `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>.json` per drain run |
| **Read-side grader** | A command reads the write-side records and emits a verdict (numeric grade, OK/WATCH/KILL, pass/fail). | The `grade` command — completion %, trend, failure tuples, verdict against kill condition |

Both halves are user stories *in the initiative itself*. You build the grader as part of the work, not as an afterthought at cycle close.

## Why this is the right default

Three things it gives you that manual inspection doesn't:

1. **Cheap to re-run.** Cycle close becomes one command, not an afternoon of reading.
2. **Honest by construction.** If you have to write the grader, you can't fudge the KR. The grader returns a verdict whether you like it or not.
3. **Filler-KR filter.** Filler KRs don't admit graders. "We will document this" has no grader. "Skill exists and has all anatomy sections" does — `grep -c '^## ' SKILL.md` is the grader. *If you can't describe the grader command in one sentence, the KR is filler.*

That last one is operationally strong — it's a sharper version of rule 5d than what's currently in ABA-191.

## How it slots into Layer 2

Each of the four Layer 2 templates gets a `grader:` sub-field, preferred form:

| Layer 1 | Grader shape |
|---|---|
| Correctness | script that runs golden-path inputs and diffs against expected output → exit code + diff summary |
| Outcome | script that scans the resulting artefacts (Linear issues, files, logs) and counts the observable behaviour → numeric grade |
| Maintenance | one-liner that checks the structural cap (`wc -l`, `grep -c`, file count) → pass/fail |
| Discipline | one-liner that asserts artefact existence + section count + no-TBD → pass/fail |

And: **building the grader is in scope for the initiative.** It shows up as one or two user stories — the write-side schema + the read-side command — exactly like the drain-cycle example.

## What this changes about ABA-191

Two additions:

1. **Rule 5** gets a new sub-rule (or rule 5d expands): "Every KR must name its grader — a command/script/query that emits a verdict against the target. If the KR's grader can't be described in one sentence, the KR fails 5d. Building the grader is in the initiative's scope unless explicitly waived."
2. **`references/kr-quality-templates.md`** gets a section "The grader-backed KR pattern" with the two-part shape, the drain-cycle example as the worked case, and a "when manual grading is OK" carve-out (one-shot work, throwaway, or KRs where the verdict really is qualitative — though those should be rare).

Want me to fold both into ABA-191 now, or split #1 into a sibling ticket so the rubric change and the new reference file land as separate units of work?

|\n|---|---|\n| **Write-side artefact** | Every relevant event/run/state-change emits a structured record at a known location with a known schema. |\n| **Read-side grader** | A command reads the across recent cycles and prints a grade — per-cycle completion %, trend across the last N cycles, recurrent failure-mode tuples, and a verdict against the kill condition (OK / WATCH / KILL) — so I can decide whether to ship-as-is, iterate, or walk away, without re-reading JSON by hand.*\n\n## Why this is the right default\n\n1. **Cheap to re-run.** Cycle close becomes one command, not an afternoon.\n2. **Honest by construction.** If you have to write the grader, you can't fudge the KR. The grader returns a verdict whether you like it or not.\n3. **Filler-KR filter.** Filler KRs don't admit graders. \"We will document this\" has no grader. \"Skill exists and has all anatomy sections\" does — `grep -c '^## ' SKILL.md` is the grader. **If you can't describe the grader command in one sentence, the KR is filler.**\n\nThat third property is the load-bearing one: it's a sharper rule than ABA-191's rule 5b/5c/5d combined.\n\n## Layer 2 — each template gets a `grader:` sub-field\n\nABA-191 introduces 4 Layer 2 templates (golden-path test, self-trial protocol, structural cap + revisit, artefact-exists). This ticket extends each with a `grader:` sub-field, with a preferred shape:\n\n| Layer 1 | Grader shape |\n|---|---|\n| **Correctness** | script that runs golden-path inputs and diffs against expected output → exit code + diff summary |\n| **Outcome** | script that scans resulting artefacts (Linear issues, files, logs) and counts observable behaviour → numeric grade |\n| **Maintenance** | one-liner that checks structural caps (`wc -l`, `grep -c`, file count) → pass/fail |\n| **Discipline** | one-liner that asserts artefact existence + section count + no-TBD → pass/fail |\n\n## Change — rubric rule #5\n\nExtend rule 5d (introduced by ABA-191) or add a new sub-rule:\n\n* **5d (extended) — KR is grader-backed.** Each KR's `source:` points at a command/script/query that emits a verdict against the target. The grader command must be describable in one sentence at draft time. Building the grader (write-side schema + read-side command) is in the initiative's scope — included as one or two user stories — unless explicitly waived under the carve-out below.\n\n### Manual-grading carve-out\n\nA KR may be manually graded (no grader required) only when:\n\n* The initiative is genuinely one-shot or throwaway (and the initiative type field reflects that), or\n* The KR's verdict is irreducibly qualitative (e.g. \"the prose reads naturally\") and no automatable proxy is honest.\n\nThe carve-out is the exception, not the default. CI is out of scope for the skill pack).\n* Changes to `references/initiative-types.md` to bake graders into per-type playbooks (could be a follow-up).\n\n## Why this is small\n\nOne rule extension in the skill, one section appended to a reference file. The pattern itself is already validated in the wild (drain-cycle)."
