Work on Linear issue ABA-191:

<issue identifier="ABA-191">
<title>Tighten /initiative-shape KR-quality gate to reject filler KRs</title>
<description>
## Problem

The `/initiative-shape` Step 6.5 rubric requires ≥3 KRs (rule #4). In real use, this floor produces a failure mode the rubric doesn't catch: when an initiative only has 2 *load-bearing* dimensions, the user pads to 3 with a filler KR just to clear the gate. The padded KR passes rule #5 ("observable state") and rule #6 ("sub-fields filled") at face value, but the KR is meaningless and the post-cycle review degrades.

The ≥3 floor itself is load-bearing — each of the 6 type playbooks defines a 3-KR minimum that maps to grading dimensions. Loosening the count would silently allow under-specified initiatives. The right fix is to make the **quality** gate strong enough that filler is rejected at draft time, so the user is forced to either find a real 3rd dimension or descope the initiative.

## Scope context — personal projects only

All initiatives in this workspace are personal projects with no production traffic, no real-user funnel, and no on-call. Layer 1 dimensions and Layer 2 templates are calibrated for that scale. SRE SLO machinery (availability/latency/error budgets), DORA four keys (deployment frequency/MTTR), and product funnels (AARRR) are explicitly **not** the reference frameworks here — they assume operational context that doesn't exist.

What does matter for personal projects:

1. Good goals that prioritize work effectively.
2. Tools to measure whether what's being built is working as intended — verifiable by one person, on a laptop, without dashboards or telemetry.

## Filler patterns the new rules must catch

1. **Target = baseline.** "baseline 100%, target 100%" — zero delta, no information.
2. **Two KRs measuring the same dimension.** KR1 "test pass rate ≥95%" + KR3 "test coverage ≥90%" — both = "tests are good."
3. **KR is the Goal restated.** Goal: "decision quality improves." KR: "decision quality improves."
4. **Sub-field points nowhere.** `source: the logs` / `baseline: currently unknown` / `target: better than today`.
5. **Meta-KRs added for gate compliance.** "We will document" / "we will write tests" added to hit the count, not load-bearing pre-gate.

## Layer 1 — the four personal-project dimensions (used by rule 5b)

| Dimension | What it answers | When it's load-bearing |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Correctness** | does the thing produce the intended output on the cases I care about | always, for any build |
| **Outcome / behaviour change** | does the thing I built actually change what I do or how I work | most product-shaped initiatives (Types 2, 4, 5, 6 from `references/initiative-types.md`) |
| **Maintenance / future-me cost** | can I come back in 30 days and still navigate this | always — solo means future-me pays the whole tax |
| **Discipline / completion** | did the artefacts get finished, or did this half-ship | always — solo means nobody else will finish it |

Dropped from the original brainstormed list: performance, operability-as-SLO, adoption-as-funnel, calibration, coverage, no-silent-failure. They were enterprise-shaped sub-dimensions; they re-enter only as per-initiative exceptions when a specific initiative genuinely ships runtime traffic.

## Layer 2 — measurement templates (one per Layer 1 dimension)

Lives in a new reference file `references/kr-quality-templates.md`, cited from rule 5 in the skill.

| Layer 1 | Template | What a good KR includes | Example |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| **Correctness** | Golden-path test | named input(s) → named expected output → how it's verified (script run, snapshot diff, eyeball check) → judged-by date | "Run `/initiative-shape` against 5 fixture inputs in `fixtures/`; all 5 produce the 6-field structure verified by a checked-in snapshot diff; judged at cycle close" |
| **Outcome** | Self-trial protocol | the next N times I hit situation X, observable behaviour Y happens (or doesn't); judged by inspecting the resulting artefacts | "Next 5 initiatives shaped after this lands: zero filler KRs, judged by reading the Linear project descriptions" |
| **Maintenance** | Structural cap + revisit gate | size/shape cap (LOC, section count, file count) stays within a stated bound; revisit-at-N-days check that I can still navigate without re-reading | "`SKILL.md` stays ≤ 300 lines; in 30 days I can answer 'where does rule 5c live' from memory" |
| **Discipline** | Artefact-exists + sections-complete | named artefact exists, named sections populated, no TBD/placeholder, predecessor/supersedes declared if applicable | "<issue id="0b00aafe-b668-47db-8768-31eca82ad6f4">ABA-191</issue> closes only after rule 5 has 5a/5b/5c/5d each with a 1-line pass condition, zero TBDs, and the rationalisations row added" |

Every template above is verifiable by one person, locally, without instrumentation. That's the constraint.

## Change — rubric rule #5

Replace the current single-row rule with four sub-checks:

* **5a. No degenerate KRs.** `target ≠ baseline` either numerically or by state change.
* **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** No two KRs share the same Layer 1 dimension. Default vocabulary: correctness / outcome / maintenance / discipline (see `references/kr-quality-templates.md`). A KR may invoke an out-of-default dimension (e.g. performance) only when the initiative genuinely has that property.
* **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it.
* **5d. KR matches its Layer 2 template.** For each KR, name which Layer 1 dimension it sits in and confirm it matches the template in `references/kr-quality-templates.md`.
* **5e. Existing language ban.** Keep the ban on "improve", "better", "enhance", and arbitrary count thresholds without a quality dimension.

## Change — rubric rule #6

Extend the placeholder ban list: also reject `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`. Require `source` to point to a concrete artefact: file path, directory, Linear query, log query, cached report URL, or metric name + system.

## Change — rationalisations table

Add a row covering the "I need a 3rd KR — let me add documentation / tests / logging" pattern, with the rebuttal "if the 3rd KR only exists to clear the gate, reshape or descope — don't pad."

## New reference file — `references/kr-quality-templates.md`

Single new file. Contains:

* The 4 Layer 1 dimensions with one-line definitions.
* The 4 Layer 2 templates above, each with: when it applies, what fields it requires, one good example, one bad example.
* A short "out of scope for personal projects" note naming the dimensions we deliberately don't use as defaults (performance, operability-as-SLO, adoption-as-funnel).

## Acceptance criteria

* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #5 enumerates 5a/5b/5c/5d/5e with concrete pass conditions.
* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #6 has the extended placeholder ban list and the concrete-artefact requirement for `source`.
* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rationalisations table has the new "padding to 3" row.
* Step 6.5 failure-report instruction names sub-rules (5a/5b/5c/5d/5e) when failing.
* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` exists with the 4 Layer 1 dimensions + 4 Layer 2 templates + out-of-scope note.
* Skill rule 5b and 5d cite `references/kr-quality-templates.md`.
* Manual smoke test against each of the 5 filler patterns: re-run `/initiative-shape` with a draft that exhibits the pattern; confirm the gate rejects it with a pointer to the specific sub-rule.
* Manual smoke test against a legitimate 3-KR draft (across all 4 Layer 1 dimensions): confirm it still passes.

## Files

* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` — rubric rules #5 and #6 in Step 6.5, plus the rationalisations table around lines 200-210.
* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` — new file.

## Out of scope

* Changing rule #4 (count range stays `≥ 3 and ≤ 5`).
* Changing the default KR count (still 3).
* Changing type playbooks in `references/initiative-types.md`.
* Edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md`.
* Adding a "waive the floor with justification" override path.
* Importing SRE SLO / DORA / AARRR vocabulary as defaults. They can be cited for a single initiative that genuinely needs them, but they are not the default Layer 1 vocabulary.

## Background

Originally filed after the user hit filler-KR pain on recent projects (padding to 3 KRs just to clear the gate). Initial direction was to loosen the floor from ≥3 to ≥2; after reviewing the rationale for the ≥3 floor (multi-dimensional grading; type playbooks all define 3-KR load-bearing minimums), the direction pivoted to strengthening the quality check instead. Floor stays at 3.

A follow-up review (same session) collapsed the 10-dimension brainstorm to the 4 personal-project dimensions above, dropping SRE/DORA/AARRR machinery as misapplied at this scale. Layer 2 templates were added so each Layer 1 dimension has a known-good measurement form verifiable without instrumentation.
</description>

<project name="Initiative quality - type-aware OKRs with KRs">Improve /initiative-shape so it produces type-aware OKR-shaped Linear initiatives with 3 KRs each (baseline+target+window+source), a kill condition, and a project type tag — verifiable by system inspection.</project>

42	
43	# Initiative shape
44	
45	## Purpose
46	
47	initiative-shape is the entry point for creating a new initiative. It takes a vague idea — a sentence, a direction, a problem — and shapes it into a properly formed Linear project with a goal sentence (Objective), 3 measurable key results (each with baseline / target / window / source and a committed/aspirational tag), affected repos, bounded appetite, kill condition, and project type. The shaped initiative is then created in Linear.
48	
49	The skill exists because initiatives shaped without key results become repo-aliased backlogs, and backlogs without observable outcomes don't drive decisions. The Objective and KRs must be defined before the work begins — not inferred once the issues are closed (Rules P2, A3, C1, agentic Principle 3).
50	
51	## When to use
52	
53	- Starting any body of work that will span 5 or more issues.
54	- Converting an existing repo-project into a properly formed initiative.
55	- Preparing 3 initiatives for an upcoming cycle — run this once per initiative.
56	- When "I want to work on X" and X is clearly bigger than a single issue or bug fix.
57	
58	## When not to use
59	
60	- **Single-issue, bug, or KTLO work** — create the issue directly and put it in the ops slot. The ops slot has no goal/KR requirement.
61	- **Unvalidated ideas that haven't cleared idea-triage** — run `idea-triage` first if you're unsure the problem is worth pursuing at all.
62	- **Scoping an already-formed initiative** — use `planning-and-task-breakdown` once goal + key results are confirmed.
63	
64	## Inputs
65	
66	The vague idea in any form: a sentence, a project name, a direction, a problem statement fragment. The skill probes for everything else — do not require the user to pre-format anything.
67	
68	Optional: a list of existing open issues the user expects to belong to this initiative.
69	
70	## Outputs
71	
72	A Linear project (via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_project`) whose description follows the six-field initiative format: Goal / Key results / Affected repos / Appetite / Kill condition / Project type. Each KR carries five sub-fields (state, baseline, target, window, source) and a `[committed|aspirational]` tag. The project starts in Planned state — it does not enter a cycle until cycle planning.
73	
74	## Workflow
75	
76	**1. Capture the raw idea.**
77	Write it down verbatim. Do not reframe it yet.
78	
79	**2. [GATE] Problem or solution?**
80	Read the raw idea. Is it framed as something to build ("add X", "integrate Y") or a problem to solve ("users can't Z", "the model output isn't usable")? If solution, probe: "What goes wrong if we don't build this?" If the underlying problem can't be articulated, the initiative is not ready. Return for clarification; do not proceed.
81	
82	**2.5. Probe — project type.**
83	Before asking outcome questions, capture which of the six project types this initiative belongs to. The type sets the Objective shape and the rubric the KRs are judged against — a methodology skill pack and a personal product can both pass cycle close, but they pass on different KRs, because their theories of success are different.
84	
85	Ask:
86	
87	> "Which type? `1` methodology skill pack · `2` personal product · `3` utility skill pack · `4` research/thesis · `5` equity research · `6` production / customer-facing."
88	
89	One-paragraph descriptions of each type are in `references/initiative-types.md` — load it if the user wants the longer form. If the user can't pick a single type, the initiative likely contains two outcomes — flag and split before proceeding.
90	
91	**3. Probe — outcome questions.**
92	
93	**Load the type-specific playbook first.** Open `references/initiative-types.md` and read the Objective shape, default KR mix, and anti-patterns for the type chosen at Step 2.5. The KR mix names which leading + lagging dimensions this initiative is scored along — propose KRs from those dimensions rather than from a blank page.
94	
95	Default KR mix by type:
96	
97	- **Type 1 (methodology skill pack)** — invocation-rate KR (leading) + decision-quality KR (lagging) + anti-output guard KR (committed). See playbook for a worked example on `/initiative-shape` itself.
98	- **Type 2 (personal product, single-user)** — correctness KR (lagging) + no-silent-failure KR (committed) + maintenance-burden KR (committed). Availability KR optional 4th when scheduled-job completion is the central guarantee. Growth, MAU, retention KRs are categorically inapplicable — there is one user.
99	- **Type 3 (utility skill pack)** — first-shot correctness KR (aspirational, lagging) + coverage KR (committed) + use-log discipline KR (committed). Domain-specific quality KR (pricing accuracy, print acceptance, sale outcome) optional 4th when the pack has a quantifiable downstream signal. Use-log discipline is non-negotiable — every invocation must be logged at the moment of use to a named location (e.g. `listings/`, `outputs/`, `runs/`) with input, generated artefact, final-posted artefact, and edit-or-not outcome, or the lagging first-shot correctness KR is ungradable. Time-to-result KRs are usually vanity for Type 3 — only include if speed is the named bottleneck.
100	- **Type 4 (research / thesis-driven)** — knowledge-claim KR (committed) + critique-survivability KR (aspirational, lagging) + source-discipline KR (committed). Adoption KR (paying audience engagement, validated buyer interest) is a later-stage 4th — defer until critique-survivability has held. Readership of an undefended thesis is noise; "publish N essays" or "N readers" KRs are output, not defensibility. See Type 4 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`.
101	- **Type 5 (equity research tooling)** — pre-registration KR (committed) + calibration KR (aspirational, lagging) + hit-rate KR (aspirational, lagging). Decision-quality KR (per-call postmortem) optional 4th when the cycle window covers prior 12-month outcomes. Pre-registration is non-negotiable — every BUY/WATCH/AVOID call must be written to a dated, immutable artefact (cached `reports/TICKER_YYYYMMDD.json` + git history) before outcomes are visible, or the lagging KRs are ungradable. This is the Tetlock standard: calibration over a portfolio of pre-registered calls beats accuracy on any single name.
102	- **Type 6 (production / customer-facing)** — activation/retention/conversion KR (aspirational, lagging) + quality-pair KR (committed — NPS, p95 latency, error rate, or support volume per user) + telemetry-discipline KR (committed). Growth without a paired quality counterweight is the canonical failure mode — every value KR must have a paired quality KR. Telemetry must be in place before the cycle starts (no telemetry = no Type 6 KR); "ship feature X to 100% of users" and un-paired MAU/DAU KRs are Cagan's canonical output-disguised-as-outcome. See Type 6 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`.
103	
104	Then ask explicitly. Do not infer. Wait for a response before synthesising.
105	
106	- **Who is affected?** Which users, operators, or contexts does this problem touch?
107	- **What's the negative outcome if this isn't solved?** What task fails, what decision can't be made, what workflow breaks?
108	- **What 3 observable states would tell you it worked?** (Cap at 5; default 3 — per Wodtke.) Each one should be something a future agent can verify by looking at the system — a binary pass/fail, a fitness function firing, or a measurable delta. Avoid "improve X" / "better Y" language and avoid arbitrary "run N times" thresholds. **For all six types, propose the default mix from the playbook (Type 1: invocation-rate + decision-quality + anti-output guard; Type 2: correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden, with availability as an optional 4th; Type 3: first-shot correctness + coverage + use-log discipline, with domain-specific quality as an optional 4th — and never omit the use-log discipline KR for Type 3, and require the use-log location be named as a file path or directory in the KR's source field; Type 4: knowledge-claim + critique-survivability + source-discipline, with adoption as a later-stage 4th — defer adoption KRs until critique-survivability has held; Type 5: pre-registration + calibration + hit-rate, with decision-quality postmortem as an optional 4th — and never omit the pre-registration KR for Type 5; Type 6: activation/retention/conversion + quality-pair + telemetry-discipline — every value KR must have a paired quality KR, and telemetry must be in place before the cycle starts) and ask the user to confirm or reshape — do not start from a blank page when a playbook applies.**
109	- **For each KR, fill all four sub-fields:**
110	  - **baseline** — current value or state (if unknown, the first issue in the initiative is to measure it)
111	  - **target** — value or state we expect at the end
112	  - **window** — when this is judged ("by end of cycle", "across next 4 cycles", "within 30 days")
113	  - **source** — where the evidence will live (a file path, a Linear query, a log, a cached report)
114	  - And tag each KR `[committed]` (must hit 1.0 — operability, no-silent-failure, baseline tracking) or `[aspirational]` (0.6–0.7 is success — outcome KRs, behaviour change, forecast calibration). A mixed OKR with 1–2 committed + 1–2 aspirational is the common shape.
115	- **Which repos does this touch?** Name them. Cross-repo scope is allowed; name it explicitly.
116	
117	**4. Probe — scope and kill condition.**
118	Two separate questions:
119	
120	- **Appetite — how many issues?** Guide: 5 ≈ small (1–2 days), 10 ≈ medium (full cycle slot), 15 ≈ large (fills the whole cycle). If the answer exceeds 15, the initiative needs splitting — flag this now.
121	- **Kill condition — when do we stop?** Name the observable state that says "the bet didn't work, walk away." An initiative with KRs but no kill condition becomes a zombie. Phrasing: "If [KR] fails for [N] consecutive cycles" / "If we ship [X] and [baseline metric] doesn't move" / "If we learn [Y] in research".
122	
123	**5. Synthesise into initiative format.**
124	Draft the six fields (OKR-shaped — Goal is the Objective; Key results are 3 observable states with full sub-field discipline). Use the template in the next section.
125	
126	Each KR's state should be one of: a binary pass/fail ("X works on the common path with no manual intervention"), a fitness function firing ("the guard fails the run loudly when Y"), or a measurable delta ("token footprint drops vs baseline"). No "improve X" / "better Y" / "run N times" language. Every KR must have all four sub-fields (baseline / target / window / source) and a `[committed|aspirational]` tag.
127	
128	Present the draft. Do not create the Linear project yet.
129	
130	**6. [GATE] User confirms the draft.**
131	Ask explicitly: "Does this capture the initiative correctly? Any changes to the Objective, the Key results, kill condition, or project type before I create the project?" Do not proceed until confirmed. Fixing a wrong problem statement, vague KR, or missing kill condition here takes one minute; fixing it mid-cycle costs days.
132	
133	**6.5. [GATE] Verification rubric — 10 cross-cutting rules.**
134	Run the following checks against the confirmed draft. Each rule is a binary pass/fail. Any failure returns the draft to Step 5 for repair — the Linear project is NOT created until all 10 pass.
135	
136	| # | Rule | Pass condition |
137	|---|---|---|
138	| 1 | All six fields present | Goal / Key results / Affected repos / Appetite / Kill condition / Project type are all non-empty |
139	| 2 | Goal names who + outcome | Sentence contains an affected party and a desired outcome — no solution verb ("build", "add", "integrate", "ship") |
140	| 3 | Initiative name is a goal/problem | Name does not open with a solution verb ("Build X", "Add Y", "Integrate Z") |
141	| 4 | At least 3 KRs, no more than 5 | KR count ≥ 3 and ≤ 5 |
142	| 5 | Every KR is an observable state | No KR contains "improve", "better", "enhance", or arbitrary count thresholds without a correctness or quality dimension |
143	| 6 | Every KR has all four sub-fields | Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank or "TBD" |
144	| 7 | Every KR is tagged | Each KR carries exactly one of `[committed]` or `[aspirational]` |
145	| 8 | Kill condition is present | Non-empty; names an observable state, not a vague clause ("if it doesn't work") |
146	| 9 | Project type is canonical | One of: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6 — not free text |
147	| 10 | Appetite is in issues | Expressed as "~N issues" — not days, weeks, or sprints |
148	
149	Report each failing rule by number with the specific text that triggered the failure. When all 10 pass, proceed to Step 7.
150	
151	**7. Create the Linear project.**
152	Call `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_project` with:
153	- `name`: goal or problem label — not a solution name, not a repo name
154	- `description`: the six-field initiative format (see template below)
155	- Status: Planned
156	
157	Confirm the project URL and share it.
158	
159	**8. Optional: assign known issues.**
160	If the user listed existing issues for this initiative, list them and offer to reassign them to the new project via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue`. Assign only the ones the user confirms.
161	
162	## Initiative description template
163	
164	```markdown
165	**Goal:** For [who], we want to [solve problem / achieve outcome].
166	
167	**Key results:**
168	
169	**KR1 [committed|aspirational]** — [observable state — binary pass/fail, fitness function firing, or measurable delta]
170	- baseline: [current value/state]
171	- target:   [target value/state]
172	- window:   [time frame]
173	- source:   [where the evidence will live — file path, Linear query, log, cached report]
174	
175	**KR2 [committed|aspirational]** — [observable state]
176	- baseline: ...
177	- target:   ...
178	- window:   ...
179	- source:   ...
180	
181	**KR3 [committed|aspirational]** — [observable state]
182	- baseline: ...
183	- target:   ...
184	- window:   ...
185	- source:   ...
186	
187	(3 KRs default; cap at 5. When the key results hold — or are definitively ruled out — the initiative is Done.)
188	
189	**Affected repos:** [list]
190	
191	**Appetite:** ~[N] issues
192	
193	**Kill condition:** [the observable state that says "stop pursuing this Objective"]
194	
195	**Project type:** [1: methodology | 2: personal product | 3: utility skill pack | 4: research/thesis | 5: equity research | 6: production]
196	```
197	
198	## Common rationalisations
199	
200	| Rationalisation | Rebuttal |
201	|---|---|
202	| "I know what the goal is — I don't need to write it down." | The KRs aren't for you right now; they're for the agent in the next session who has no memory of this conversation. Write them down. |
203	| "The key results will be obvious once the work is done." | Defining them after the work is done is how "shipped = done" creeps in. KRs are what convert a list of closed issues into an achieved outcome. |
204	| "One KR is enough — the goal sentence covers the rest." | One KR collapses easily into a single arbitrary threshold. 3 KRs force you to name the dimensions that actually matter (correctness, no-blocking, no-silent-failure, speed) — and that's the discipline. |
205	| "I don't know the baseline — I'll add it later." | If baseline isn't known, the first issue in the initiative is to measure it. A KR without a baseline is an aspiration, not a result — you can't grade it at cycle close. |
206	| "The target is obvious from the goal — I don't need to spell it out separately." | The target is what makes the KR scoreable. Without it, "we'll know it when we see it" replaces a binary pass/fail, and the post-launch review degrades to vibes. |
207	| "I don't need a window — we'll just keep at it until it lands." | Without a window, a KR cannot be closed. Open-ended KRs become zombies — they neither succeed nor fail, they just live on the project page forever. |
208	| "The source of evidence is obvious — it's just whether the feature works." | Name the artefact. A file path, a Linear query, a log line, a cached report. If you can't name where the evidence lives, the KR isn't inspectable — and an uninspectable KR is a feeling, not a result. |
209	| "Why a kill condition? We're going to make this work." | Most bets don't pay off on the first try. The kill condition is what protects the next quarter from a sunk-cost zombie. Naming it up front costs one sentence; not naming it costs an entire cycle. |
210	| "Project type is bureaucratic — we know what kind of thing this is." | The field exists so the next agent — or you in three weeks — can apply the right rubric without re-deriving the taxonomy. It's a one-token tag and a downstream skill needs it. |
211	| "We just need to author N new skills this cycle — the pack is the deliverable." | For methodology skill packs (Type 1), volume of skills authored is output, not outcome. Success is invocation accuracy at the decision moment + decision quality when invoked. Reshape into invocation-rate + decision-quality KRs — see Type 1 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`. |
212	| "Improving skill quality this cycle is enough — we don't need a KR for it." | "Improve quality" fails Wodtke's weekly-trackable test. Reshape into a decision-quality KR: in ≥X of last N sampled invocations, the skill surfaced an issue or produced an artefact that would otherwise not have appeared. Now it has a sample, target, and window — and can be graded at cycle close. |
213	| "Let's add user registration so we can onboard people to [personal product]." | Type 2 is single-user by definition — no users to register. If signup is genuinely the work, the initiative is Type 6 (production / customer-facing), not Type 2 — re-classify before proceeding. |
214	| "Let's set a KR to increase MAU / DAU / retention on the personal app." | Categorically inapplicable to Type 2. There is one user. The dimensions that matter are correctness, no-silent-failure, and maintenance burden — growth KRs belong to Type 6. See Type 2 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`. |
215	| "We just need to add more skills to the pack this cycle — wider coverage is the win." | For utility skill packs (Type 3), volume of skills is output, not outcome. Success is first-shot correctness of the artefact and coverage of the categories the user actually encounters end-to-end. Five more skills with no use-log produce zero graded improvement. Reshape into first-shot correctness + coverage KRs over the categories actually hit — see Type 3 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`. |
216	| "Let's run `/resell-au` N times this cycle as a usage drive." | Activity vanity. Twenty invocations with bad outputs is failure, not progress. Type 3 grades on the quality of each artefact, not the count of them. Reshape into a first-shot correctness KR ("≥X of next N posted without text edits") over the invocations you would have made anyway. |
217	| "Skip the use-log — I'll remember which invocations went well." | Memory is selective recall of the wins. Without immutable per-invocation capture at the moment of use — input, generated artefact, final-posted artefact, edit-or-not outcome — the lagging first-shot correctness KR collapses into a vibe check. For Type 3, the use-log discipline KR is non-negotiable, parallel to Type 5's pre-registration KR. Name the use-log location as a file path or directory in the KR's source field. |
218	| "Let's run `/stock-screen` on a bunch of new tickers this cycle." | Type 5 grades on calibration, not throughput. Twenty new screens with no pre-registration produce twenty unrecorded opinions, not twenty results. Reshape into a pre-registration KR over the names you actually call — see Type 5 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`. |
219	| "We'll just check that BUY calls outperform the S&P over the next year." | Un-pre-registered and cherry-pickable. Without a dated, immutable set of names locked at call time, "outperform" becomes whatever cherry-picked window the analyst chooses post-hoc. Reshape into a hit-rate KR over the explicit pre-registered cohort and add a pre-registration KR (committed) to lock the cohort. |
220	| "Skip the pre-registration KR for the equity initiative — we already write reports per call." | Writing reports is not pre-registration. Pre-registration means the call is dated, committed to git, and immutable before the 12-month window closes. Without the immutability check, the lagging calibration and hit-rate KRs are arguments about what was meant, not checks of what was claimed (Tetlock). For Type 5, the pre-registration KR is non-negotiable. |
221	| "We just need to publish N essays this cycle to ground the thesis." | For research/thesis-driven (Type 4), essay count is output, not defensibility. A thesis with 20 unsourced essays is weaker than one with 3 source-backed claims that hold under critique. Reshape into knowledge-claim + critique-survivability KRs — see Type 4 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`. |
222	| "Let's set a KR for N readers / N newsletter subscribers on the thesis." | Adoption masquerading as outcome on an undefended thesis. Readership of a thesis that hasn't cleared expert critique is noise — it tells you the headline is catchy, not that the assertions are right. Defer adoption KRs until critique-survivability has held; otherwise the wrong audience signal entrenches a wrong thesis. |
223	| "Just ship feature X to 100% of users this cycle as the win." | Cagan's canonical output-disguised-as-outcome for Type 6. Rollout is implementation; the OKR scores whether the customer behaviour the feature was supposed to change actually moved. Reshape into a behaviour-change KR (activation/retention/conversion) with a paired quality KR — see Type 6 playbook in `references/initiative-types.md`. |
224	| "Let's set a KR to increase MAU / DAU." | Type 6 value KR without a paired quality KR is half a KR. MAU can be inflated by re-engagement emails that crater NPS, or by registration prompts that bloat the denominator. Every growth KR is paired with a quality counterweight (NPS, retention, churn, support-per-user) — the pair is what makes the vanity-gain trade visible. |
225	| "This is too big for 15 issues but it's one coherent thing." | Split by outcome: which key result do you want first? That's one initiative. The rest follow. |
226	| "I'll sort out the KRs after we create the project." | Step 6 is a gate. The project doesn't get created until the KRs (with all sub-fields) and kill condition are confirmed. |
227	| "The affected repo is obvious — we only work in one repo here." | Name it anyway. The field exists for cross-repo legibility, not to teach you something you don't know. |
228	
229	## Red flags
230	
231	- A KR says "improve X" or "better Y" with no observable state to verify.
232	- KRs are arbitrary "run N times" thresholds rather than common-path correctness, no-silent-failure, or measurable-delta states.
233	- Only 1 or 2 KRs — the initiative is probably under-specified along the dimensions that actually matter (default 3; cap 5).
234	- A KR is missing one or more of baseline / target / window / source.
235	- A KR is missing its `[committed]` or `[aspirational]` tag — the rubric for grading it at cycle close is undefined.
236	- No kill condition — the initiative has no defined off-ramp and will become a zombie when the bet doesn't pay off.
237	- The Project type field is missing or set to a free-text label that doesn't match the six-type taxonomy.
238	- For Type 1 (methodology skill pack), every KR scores artefact volume (skills authored, lines written, files edited) — no invocation-rate (leading) or decision-quality (lagging) KR. The OKR is then measuring output, not outcome.
239	- For Type 2 (personal product, single-user), any KR targets growth metrics (MAU, DAU, retention, signups) or feature volume — the type was misclassified, or growth metrics were imported from a product model that doesn't apply.
240	- For Type 3 (utility skill pack), every KR scores skill volume ("N skills authored") or invocation count ("run N times this cycle") — the OKR is measuring output, not utility. Reshape into first-shot correctness (≤ one edit before posting/printing) and coverage (zero unhandled categories) KRs.
241	- For Type 3, no use-log discipline KR (committed) and no source location named as a file path or directory for the first-shot correctness KR — the lagging KR is ungradable because there's no immutable per-invocation record to grade against.
242	- For Type 5 (equity research tooling), no pre-registration KR (committed) — every BUY/WATCH/AVOID call must be logged to a dated, immutable artefact (cached `reports/TICKER_YYYYMMDD.json` + git history) before outcomes are visible, or the lagging calibration and hit-rate KRs are ungradable by construction.
243	- For Type 5, KRs target throughput ("run `/stock-screen` on N tickers", "produce N reports") or un-pre-registered outperformance ("BUY calls beat the S&P") — Type 5 grades on calibration of dated, locked cohorts, not on volume of analyses or post-hoc cherry-picked windows.
244	- For Type 4 (research / thesis-driven), every KR scores essay count, reader count, or newsletter subscribers — no knowledge-claim or source-discipline KR. The OKR is measuring artefact volume or audience noise, not defensibility of the assertions.
245	- For Type 6 (production / customer-facing), a growth / conversion / retention KR has no paired quality KR — Cagan's canonical failure mode where the value KR is achieved by destroying NPS, latency, error rate, or support volume per user.
246	- For Type 6, a KR uses feature-rollout language ("ship X to 100%", "launch Y") rather than naming the customer behaviour the feature was supposed to change — the OKR is measuring delivery, not outcome.
247	- For Type 6, telemetry isn't in place before the cycle starts and there is no telemetry-discipline KR to instrument it — the value and quality KRs cannot be graded because the baseline can't be read and definitions can drift mid-cycle.
248	- The initiative name describes a solution ("Build the X feature") rather than a goal or problem.
249	- The appetite is expressed as a time duration ("2 weeks") rather than an issue count.
250	- The Linear project was created before Step 6 confirmed the draft.
251	- An initiative with > 15 issues in appetite was created without a split decision.
252	
253	## Verification / exit criteria
254	
255	The skill has run correctly when:
256	
257	1. A Linear project exists with a description containing all six canonical fields (Goal / Key results / Affected repos / Appetite / Kill condition / Project type).
258	2. The goal sentence names who is affected and what the outcome is — not a solution.
259	3. The Key results list contains 3 entries (cap 5), each an observable state (binary pass/fail, fitness function firing, or measurable delta) — no "improve X" / "better Y" / "run N times" language.
260	4. Every KR carries all four sub-fields — baseline, target, window, source — and a `[committed]` or `[aspirational]` tag.
261	5. The kill condition is present and names an observable state that says "stop pursuing this Objective."
262	6. The project type is set to one of the six values (1–6).
263	7. The appetite is expressed in issues (not days or weeks).
264	8. The user confirmed the draft before the project was created (Step 6 gate honoured).
265	9. For type=1 (methodology skill pack), at least one KR is an invocation-rate (leading) indicator and at least one is a decision-quality (lagging) indicator — the OKR is not measuring only artefact volume.
266	10. For type=2 (personal product), at least one KR scores correctness (lagging) and at least one KR scores operability (no-silent-failure, maintenance-burden, or availability) — the OKR is not measuring feature volume or growth metrics that don't apply.
267	11. For type=3 (utility skill pack), at least one KR is a first-shot correctness KR (aspirational, lagging — ≥X of next N artefacts used without text edits) AND at least one KR is a use-log discipline KR (committed) naming the use-log location as a file path or directory in its source field — the OKR is gradable post-hoc from the use-log alone, without narration from the operator.
268	12. For type=4 (research / thesis-driven), at least one KR is a knowledge-claim KR (committed — N specific defensible assertions recorded in the thesis artefact) AND at least one KR is a source-discipline KR (committed — zero uncited load-bearing claims; sources file at a named path) — the OKR is gradable post-hoc from the thesis artefact alone, without narration from the author. Adoption KRs are absent or explicitly deferred until critique-survivability has held.
269	13. For type=5 (equity research tooling), at least one KR is a pre-registration KR (committed) requiring every BUY/WATCH/AVOID call to be logged to a dated, immutable artefact (cached `reports/TICKER_YYYYMMDD.json` + git history) before outcomes are visible — the OKR is gradable post-hoc by a third party from artefacts alone, without narration from the original analyst.
270	14. For type=6 (production / customer-facing), at least one KR is an activation/retention/conversion KR (aspirational, lagging — baseline → target on a named cohort) AND at least one KR is a paired quality KR (committed — NPS, p95 latency, error rate, or support volume per user, with a tolerance) AND at least one KR is a telemetry-discipline KR (committed — cohort + metric definitions recorded before cycle start with no retroactive edits). The OKR is gradable from production telemetry alone, without narration from the team.
271	
272	## References
273	
274	- `rules/linear-workflow.md` — initiative definition, lifecycle, cycle model, ops slot (source of truth; six-field shape and KR sub-field requirements)
275	- `rules/PRODUCT_RULES.md` — P2 (problems not solutions), P3 (bets), A2 (problem format), A3 (measurable success criteria — applied here as 3 KRs with baseline/target/window/source discipline), C1 (appetite)
276	- `rules/eng-principles-agentic.md` — Principle 3 (spec as seatbelt; goal must precede work)
277	- `references/initiative-types.md` — six-type taxonomy and per-type playbooks (Objective shape, default KR mix, anti-patterns, verification rubric) — loaded at Step 2.5 and Step 3
278	- Research Section 5 — 10 cross-cutting verification rules (inline copy at Step 6.5); original source: Linear document "Research and implementation plan — OKR shapes by project type", Section 5 (project `Initiative quality — type-aware OKRs with KRs`)
279	- `skills/idea-triage/SKILL.md` — upstream: run when confidence is low before committing to an initiative
280	- `skills/planning-and-task-breakdown/SKILL.md` — downstream: breaks a confirmed initiative into issues
281

{"id":"ABA-191","title":"Tighten /initiative-shape KR-quality gate to reject filler KRs","description":"## Problem\n\nThe `/initiative-shape` Step 6.5 rubric requires ≥3 KRs (rule #4). In real use, this floor produces a failure mode the rubric doesn't catch: when an initiative only has 2 *load-bearing* dimensions, the user pads to 3 with a filler KR just to clear the gate. The padded KR passes rule #5 (\"observable state\") and rule #6 (\"sub-fields filled\") at face value, but the KR is meaningless and the post-cycle review degrades.\n\nThe ≥3 floor itself is load-bearing — each of the 6 type playbooks defines a 3-KR minimum that maps to grading dimensions. Loosening the count would silently allow under-specified initiatives. The right fix is to make the **quality** gate strong enough that filler is rejected at draft time, so the user is forced to either find a real 3rd dimension or descope the initiative.\n\n## Scope context — personal projects only\n\nAll initiatives in this workspace are personal projects with no production traffic, no real-user funnel, and no on-call. Layer 1 dimensions and Layer 2 templates are calibrated for that scale. SRE SLO machinery (availability/latency/error budgets), DORA four keys (deployment frequency/MTTR), and product funnels (AARRR) are explicitly **not** the reference frameworks here — they assume operational context that doesn't exist.\n\nWhat does matter for personal projects:\n\n1. Good goals that prioritize work effectively.\n2. Tools to measure whether what's being built is working as intended — verifiable by one person, on a laptop, without dashboards or telemetry.\n\n## Filler patterns the new rules must catch\n\n1. **Target = baseline.** \"baseline 100%, target 100%\" — zero delta, no information.\n2. **Two KRs measuring the same dimension.** KR1 \"test pass rate ≥95%\" + KR3 \"test coverage ≥90%\" — both = \"tests are good.\"\n3. **KR is the Goal restated.** Goal: \"decision quality improves.\" KR: \"decision quality improves.\"\n4. **Sub-field points nowhere.** `source: the logs` / `baseline: currently unknown` / `target: better than today`.\n5. **Meta-KRs added for gate compliance.** \"We will document\" / \"we will write tests\" added to hit the count, not load-bearing pre-gate.\n\n## Layer 1 — the four personal-project dimensions (used by rule 5b)\n\n| Dimension | What it answers | When it's load-bearing |\n| -- | -- | -- |\n| **Correctness** | does the thing produce the intended output on the cases I care about | always, for any build |\n| **Outcome / behaviour change** | does the thing I built actually change what I do or how I work | most product-shaped initiatives (Types 2, 4, 5, 6 from `references/initiative-types.md`) |\n| **Maintenance / future-me cost** | can I come back in 30 days and still navigate this | always — solo means future-me pays the whole tax |\n| **Discipline / completion** | did the artefacts get finished, or did this half-ship | always — solo means nobody else will finish it |\n\nDropped from the original brainstormed list: performance, operability-as-SLO, adoption-as-funnel, calibration, coverage, no-silent-failure. They were enterprise-shaped sub-dimensions; they re-enter only as per-initiative exceptions when a specific initiative genuinely ships runtime traffic.\n\n## Layer 2 — measurement templates (one per Layer 1 dimension)\n\nLives in a new reference file `references/kr-quality-templates.md`, cited from rule 5 in the skill.\n\n| Layer 1 | Template | What a good KR includes | Example |\n| -- | -- | -- | -- |\n| **Correctness** | Golden-path test | named input(s) → named expected output → how it's verified (script run, snapshot diff, eyeball check) → judged-by date | \"Run `/initiative-shape` against 5 fixture inputs in `fixtures/`; all 5 produce the 6-field structure verified by a checked-in snapshot diff; judged at cycle close\" |\n| **Outcome** | Self-trial protocol | the next N times I hit situation X, observable behaviour Y happens (or doesn't); judged by inspecting the resulting artefacts | \"Next 5 initiatives shaped after this lands: zero filler KRs, judged by reading the Linear project descriptions\" |\n| **Maintenance** | Structural cap + revisit gate | size/shape cap (LOC, section count, file count) stays within a stated bound; revisit-at-N-days check that I can still navigate without re-reading | \"`SKILL.md` stays ≤ 300 lines; in 30 days I can answer 'where does rule 5c live' from memory\" |\n| **Discipline** | Artefact-exists + sections-complete | named artefact exists, named sections populated, no TBD/placeholder, predecessor/supersedes declared if applicable | \"<issue id=\"0b00aafe-b668-47db-8768-31eca82ad6f4\">ABA-191</issue> closes only after rule 5 has 5a/5b/5c/5d each with a 1-line pass condition, zero TBDs, and the rationalisations row added\" |\n\nEvery template above is verifiable by one person, locally, without instrumentation. That's the constraint.\n\n## Change — rubric rule #5\n\nReplace the current single-row rule with four sub-checks:\n\n* **5a. No degenerate KRs.** `target ≠ baseline` either numerically or by state change.\n* **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** No two KRs share the same Layer 1 dimension. Default vocabulary: correctness / outcome / maintenance / discipline (see `references/kr-quality-templates.md`). A KR may invoke an out-of-default dimension (e.g. performance) only when the initiative genuinely has that property.\n* **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it.\n* **5d. KR matches its Layer 2 template.** For each KR, name which Layer 1 dimension it sits in and confirm it matches the template in `references/kr-quality-templates.md`.\n* **5e. Existing language ban.** Keep the ban on \"improve\", \"better\", \"enhance\", and arbitrary count thresholds without a quality dimension.\n\n## Change — rubric rule #6\n\nExtend the placeholder ban list: also reject `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`. Require `source` to point to a concrete artefact: file path, directory, Linear query, log query, cached report URL, or metric name + system.\n\n## Change — rationalisations table\n\nAdd a row covering the \"I need a 3rd KR — let me add documentation / tests / logging\" pattern, with the rebuttal \"if the 3rd KR only exists to clear the gate, reshape or descope — don't pad.\"\n\n## New reference file — `references/kr-quality-templates.md`\n\nSingle new file. Contains:\n\n* The 4 Layer 1 dimensions with one-line definitions.\n* The 4 Layer 2 templates above, each with: when it applies, what fields it requires, one good example, one bad example.\n* A short \"out of scope for personal projects\" note naming the dimensions we deliberately don't use as defaults (performance, operability-as-SLO, adoption-as-funnel).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #5 enumerates 5a/5b/5c/5d/5e with concrete pass conditions.\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #6 has the extended placeholder ban list and the concrete-artefact requirement for `source`.\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rationalisations table has the new \"padding to 3\" row.\n* Step 6.5 failure-report instruction names sub-rules (5a/5b/5c/5d/5e) when failing.\n* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` exists with the 4 Layer 1 dimensions + 4 Layer 2 templates + out-of-scope note.\n* Skill rule 5b and 5d cite `references/kr-quality-templates.md`.\n* Manual smoke test against each of the 5 filler patterns: re-run `/initiative-shape` with a draft that exhibits the pattern; confirm the gate rejects it with a pointer to the specific sub-rule.\n* Manual smoke test against a legitimate 3-KR draft (across all 4 Layer 1 dimensions): confirm it still passes.\n\n## Files\n\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` — rubric rules #5 and #6 in Step 6.5, plus the rationalisations table around lines 200-210.\n* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` — new file.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n* Changing rule #4 (count range stays `≥ 3 and ≤ 5`).\n* Changing the default KR count (still 3).\n* Changing type playbooks in `references/initiative-types.md`.\n* Edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md`.\n* Adding a \"waive the floor with justification\" override path.\n* Importing SRE SLO / DORA / AARRR vocabulary as defaults. They can be cited for a single initiative that genuinely needs them, but they are not the default Layer 1 vocabulary.\n\n## Background\n\nOriginally filed after the user hit filler-KR pain on recent projects (padding to 3 KRs just to clear the gate).   "content": "# KR quality templates — personal-project Layer 1 / Layer 2\n\nReference cited by `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` Step 6.5 rules 5b and 5d. Use it when drafting key results to (a) pick a Layer 1 dimension and (b) shape the KR to its Layer 2 template.\n\n## Scope — personal projects only\n\nEvery initiative in this workspace is a personal project: no production traffic, no real-user funnel, no on-call rotation. The vocabulary below is calibrated for that scale. Frameworks deliberately not imported as defaults:\n\n- **SRE SLO machinery** (availability / latency / error-budget). Assumes user traffic and an on-call team.\n- **DORA four keys** (deployment frequency / lead time / change failure rate / MTTR). Assumes a deployment pipeline serving customers.\n- **AARRR product funnels** (acquisition / activation / retention / referral / revenue). Assumes a multi-user product.\n\nA single initiative can still cite one of these — but only when the initiative genuinely has that property. They are not the default Layer 1 vocabulary, and a KR drawn from them must justify why the default four don't fit.\n\n## Layer 1 — the four dimensions\n\n| Dimension | What it answers | When it's load-bearing |\n| -- | -- | -- |\n| **Correctness** | does the thing produce the intended output on the cases I care about | always, for any build |\n| **Outcome / behaviour change** | does the thing I built actually change what I do or how I work | most product-shaped initiatives (Types 2, 4, 5, 6 from `references/initiative-types.md`) |\n| **Maintenance / future-me cost** | can I come back in 30 days and still navigate this | always — solo means future-me pays the whole tax |\n| **Discipline / completion** | did the artefacts get finished, or did this half-ship | always — solo means nobody else will finish it |\n\nThe four are deliberately distinct: correctness is about the artefact's output on inputs; outcome is about whether the artefact changes operator behaviour downstream; maintenance is about cost-of-future-edit; discipline is about whether the build actually completed. A KR that conflates two of them (e.g. \"the skill works and I've used it 5 times\") is a filler signal — pick one dimension per KR.\n\n## Layer 2 — measurement templates\n\nOne template per Layer 1 dimension. Every template is verifiable by one person, locally, without instrumentation.\n\n### Correctness — Golden-path test\n\n**When it applies:** any initiative that produces a concrete artefact (skill, script, report, doc, configured tool) with verifiable output for known inputs.\n\n**Required fields:**\n- named input(s) — specific fixtures, sample tickers, sample prompts, etc.\n- named expected output — what \"correct\" looks like for each input\n- verification method — script run, snapshot diff, eyeball check against a checked-in reference\n- judged-by date — when the check is run\n\n**Good example:**\n> \"Run `/initiative-shape` against the 5 fixture inputs in `fixtures/initiative-drafts/`; all 5 produce the 6-field structure verified by `diff` against checked-in expected outputs in `fixtures/initiative-drafts/expected/`; judged at cycle close.\"\n> - baseline: `0/5` pass (fixtures don't exist yet — first issue creates them)\n> - target: `5/5` pass\n> - window: cycle close\n> - source: `fixtures/initiative-drafts/` + diff output\n\n**Bad example:**\n> \"The skill works well.\"\n> *(no named inputs, no named outputs, no verification method, no date — not gradable.)*\n\n### Outcome / behaviour change — Self-trial protocol\n\n**When it applies:** any initiative whose success is \"this changes what I do or how I work\" — methodology skills, productivity changes, decision-support tooling.\n\n**Required fields:**\n- the situation (X) where the behaviour change should occur\n- the next N occurrences over which it's judged (N is small — typically 3–10 — so the cycle window can contain it)\n- the observable behaviour (Y) that confirms or denies the change\n- inspection method — what artefact you read to grade it (cached outputs, Linear project descriptions, git logs, run logs)\n\n**Good example:**\n> \"Next 5 initiatives shaped with `/initiative-shape` after this lands: zero filler KRs in the created Linear projects, judged by re-reading each project description against rule 5a–5e.\"\n> - baseline: filler-KR rate is `~1 in 3` historically (eyeball estimate from past 9 projects)\n> - target: `0/5` filler KRs\n> - window: next 5 invocations\n> - source: Linear projects under `Initiative quality - type-aware OKRs with KRs`\n\n**Bad example:**\n> \"Initiatives will be better shaped.\"\n> *(no situation, no N, no observable behaviour, no inspection method.)*\n\n### Maintenance / future-me cost — Structural cap + revisit gate\n\n**When it applies:** any initiative producing artefacts that will be edited or re-read after a delay — skills, references, code, docs.\n\n**Required fields:**\n- size/shape cap — a stated bound: LOC, line count, section count, file count, function count\n- revisit gate — a check at N days that the artefact is still navigable (typical: \"I can answer question X from memory\" or \"I can find section Y without re-reading the whole file\")\n- judged-by date — when the revisit check is run\n\n**Good example:**\n> \"`skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` stays ≤ 320 lines; in 30 days from cycle close I can locate where rule 5c lives without grepping.\"\n> - baseline: 281 lines today\n> - target: ≤ 320 lines, navigation check passes\n> - window: 30 days from cycle close\n> - source: `wc -l skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` + a one-paragraph navigation note added to the cycle retro\n\n**Bad example:**\n> \"The skill stays maintainable.\"\n> *(no cap, no revisit gate, no date — vibe.)*\n\n### Discipline / completion — Artefact-exists + sections-complete\n\n**When it applies:** any initiative whose risk is half-shipping — the build started but the doc / test / reference / cleanup didn't land.\n\n**Required fields:**\n- named artefact (file path)\n- named sections within the artefact (e.g. \"rule 5 has 5a/5b/5c/5d/5e populated\", \"rationalisations table has the new row\")\n- zero placeholders — no `TBD`, no `unknown`, no `we'll figure it out`\n- predecessor / supersedes declaration if the artefact replaces an earlier one\n\n**Good example:**\n> \"ABA-191 closes only after `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` has rule 5 enumerated as 5a/5b/5c/5d/5e each with a 1-line pass condition, rule 6 has the extended placeholder ban list and concrete-artefact requirement, the rationalisations table has the 'padding to 3' row, and `references/kr-quality-templates.md` exists with all four templates.\"\n> - baseline: rule 5 is one row, rule 6 has the short ban list, no kr-quality-templates reference\n> - target: all four artefact changes landed\n> - window: cycle close\n> - source: `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` + `references/kr-quality-templates.md`\n\n**Bad example:**\n> \"The work will be documented.\"\n> *(no artefact path, no sections named, no completion definition.)*\n\n## Pattern for a 3-KR draft\n\nA clean 3-KR draft typically picks 3 of the 4 dimensions, not 3 of the same one. Some patterns by initiative type:\n\n- **Type 1 methodology skill pack** — outcome (invocation behaviour) + correctness (per-invocation decision quality) + discipline (artefact completion).\n- **Type 2 personal product** — correctness (named-fixture output) + outcome (does it change my workflow) + maintenance (future-me cost).\n- **Type 3 utility skill pack** — correctness (first-shot artefact quality) + discipline (use-log exists, populated) + maintenance (size cap).\n- **Type 4 research / thesis** — discipline (sources file populated, claims indexed) + outcome (next N decisions cite the thesis) + correctness (claims survive critique).\n- **Type 5 equity research** — discipline (pre-registration artefact exists, immutable) + correctness (per-call structure) + outcome (calibration trend across cohort).\n- **Type 6 production / customer-facing** — this is the type where SRE/DORA/AARRR vocabulary genuinely applies. Cite from there for the relevant KRs, but keep at least one Layer 1 dimension (typically discipline — telemetry exists, definitions locked).\n\n## Out of scope for personal projects — when not to use the defaults\n\nCategorically inappropriate as defaults at personal-project scale:\n\n- **Performance / latency SLOs** — no user traffic to budget against. Re-enter only if the initiative genuinely runs a job whose runtime gates further work.\n- **Operability as availability SLO** — no on-call. Re-enter as \"the scheduled job didn't silently fail\" via the discipline template, not as 99.X%.\n- **Adoption / growth / retention funnels** — single user. Re-enter only if the initiative is Type 6 with a real user cohort.\n\nIf the initiative genuinely needs one of these, name it explicitly as the reason the default vocabulary doesn't cover it — and write the KR against the actual property, not against a copied SRE/DORA/AARRR template.\n"
}
```

133	**6.5. [GATE] Verification rubric — 10 cross-cutting rules.**
134	Run the following checks against the confirmed draft. Each rule is a binary pass/fail. Any failure returns the draft to Step 5 for repair — the Linear project is NOT created until all 10 pass.
135	
136	| # | Rule | Pass condition |
137	|---|---|---|
138	| 1 | All six fields present | Goal / Key results / Affected repos / Appetite / Kill condition / Project type are all non-empty |
139	| 2 | Goal names who + outcome | Sentence contains an affected party and a desired outcome — no solution verb ("build", "add", "integrate", "ship") |
140	| 3 | Initiative name is a goal/problem | Name does not open with a solution verb ("Build X", "Add Y", "Integrate Z") |
141	| 4 | At least 3 KRs, no more than 5 | KR count ≥ 3 and ≤ 5 |
142	| 5a | No degenerate KRs | `target ≠ baseline` either numerically (different number) or by state change (different observable state) — bans "baseline 100%, target 100%" patterns |
143	| 5b | KRs name distinct Layer 1 dimensions | Across all KRs, no two share the same Layer 1 dimension. Default vocabulary: correctness / outcome / maintenance / discipline (see `references/kr-quality-templates.md`). An out-of-default dimension (e.g. performance) is permitted only when the initiative genuinely has that property |
144	| 5c | KRs are not Goal-restatements | Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it. Each KR must name a *measurable property of* the Goal — not the Goal itself |
145	| 5d | KR matches its Layer 2 template | For each KR, name which Layer 1 dimension it sits in and confirm it matches the template (golden-path test / self-trial protocol / structural cap + revisit gate / artefact-exists + sections-complete) in `references/kr-quality-templates.md` |
146	| 5e | Language ban | No KR contains "improve", "better", "enhance", or arbitrary "run N times" count thresholds without a correctness or quality dimension |
147	| 6 | Every KR has all four sub-fields, no placeholders, `source` is a concrete artefact | Each KR has baseline, target, window, source — none blank, none equal to `TBD`, `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`. `source` points to a concrete artefact: a file path, a directory, a Linear query, a log query (e.g. `bigquery: events WHERE ...`), a cached report URL, or a metric name + system |
148	| 7 | Every KR is tagged | Each KR carries exactly one of `[committed]` or `[aspirational]` |
149	| 8 | Kill condition is present | Non-empty; names an observable state, not a vague clause ("if it doesn't work") |

202	## Common rationalisations
203	
204	| Rationalisation | Rebuttal |
205	|---|---|
206	| "I know what the goal is — I don't need to write it down." | The KRs aren't for you right now; they're for the agent in the next session who has no memory of this conversation. Write them down. |
207	| "The key results will be obvious once the work is done." | Defining them after the work is done is how "shipped = done" creeps in. KRs are what convert a list of closed issues into an achieved outcome. |
208	| "One KR is enough — the goal sentence covers the rest." | One KR collapses easily into a single arbitrary threshold. 3 KRs force you to name the dimensions that actually matter (correctness, no-blocking, no-silent-failure, speed) — and that's the discipline. |
209	| "I need a 3rd KR — let me add one for documentation / tests / logging." | If your 3rd KR's only reason to exist is to clear rule #4, you have two real dimensions and you're padding. A meta-KR that wasn't load-bearing pre-gate is filler — and rule 5b will catch it as a duplicate of "discipline" if you already have a discipline KR. Two honest paths: (a) **reshape** — a real third Layer 1 dimension is hiding (typically maintenance or outcome, see `references/kr-quality-templates.md`); (b) **descope** — narrow the initiative until 2 KRs are honestly sufficient and the work fits an ops slot rather than an initiative slot. Padding to clear the gate is the failure mode the gate exists to catch. |
210	| "I don't know the baseline — I'll add it later." | If baseline isn't known, the first issue in the initiative is to measure it. A KR without a baseline is an aspiration, not a result — you can't grade it at cycle close. |
211	| "The target is obvious from the goal — I don't need to spell it out separately." | The target is what makes the KR scoreable. Without it, "we'll know it when we see it" replaces a binary pass/fail, and the post-launch review degrades to vibes. |
212	| "I don't need a window — we'll just keep at it until it lands." | Without a window, a KR cannot be closed. Open-ended KRs become zombies — they neither succeed nor fail, they just live on the project page forever. |
```

> AGENT

Now smoke-testing each filler pattern against the new rubric:

**Smoke test 1 — Target = baseline:** "baseline: 100%, target: 100%" → rule 5a fails ("target ≠ baseline" violated). ✓

**Smoke test 2 — Duplicate dimension:** KR1 "test pass rate ≥95%" (correctness) + KR3 "test coverage ≥90%" (correctness) → rule 5b fails (two KRs share `correctness`). ✓

**Smoke test 3 — Goal restated:** Goal "decision quality improves" + KR "decision quality improves" → rule 5c fails (KR matches Goal noun-phrase); rule 5e also fires on "improve". ✓

**Smoke test 4 — Sub-field points nowhere:** `source: the logs`, `baseline: currently unknown`, `target: better than today` → rule 6 fails (three banned placeholders). ✓

**Smoke test 5 — Meta-KR for compliance:** KR3 "We will document the initiative" → rule 5d fails (claims discipline-template but names no artefact, no sections, no completion definition); if there's already a discipline KR, rule 5b also fires. ✓

**Legitimate draft test** — Type 2 personal product with correctness (named fixtures + diff) + outcome (next-N self-trial) + maintenance (LOC cap + 30-day revisit) → all 5a/5b/5c/5d/5e pass, rule 6 passes (all sources point to files / cached reports), rules 1–4 and 7–10 pass. ✓

{"id":"ABA-191","title":"Tighten /initiative-shape KR-quality gate to reject filler KRs","description":"## Problem\n\nThe `/initiative-shape` Step 6.5 rubric requires ≥3 KRs (rule #4). In real use, this floor produces a failure mode the rubric doesn't catch: when an initiative only has 2 *load-bearing* dimensions, the user pads to 3 with a filler KR just to clear the gate. The padded KR passes rule #5 (\"observable state\") and rule #6 (\"sub-fields filled\") at face value, but the KR is meaningless and the post-cycle review degrades.\n\nThe ≥3 floor itself is load-bearing — each of the 6 type playbooks defines a 3-KR minimum that maps to grading dimensions. Loosening the count would silently allow under-specified initiatives. The right fix is to make the **quality** gate strong enough that filler is rejected at draft time, so the user is forced to either find a real 3rd dimension or descope the initiative.\n\n## Scope context — personal projects only\n\nAll initiatives in this workspace are personal projects with no production traffic, no real-user funnel, and no on-call. Layer 1 dimensions and Layer 2 templates are calibrated for that scale. SRE SLO machinery (availability/latency/error budgets), DORA four keys (deployment frequency/MTTR), and product funnels (AARRR) are explicitly **not** the reference frameworks here — they assume operational context that doesn't exist.\n\nWhat does matter for personal projects:\n\n1. Good goals that prioritize work effectively.\n2. Tools to measure whether what's being built is working as intended — verifiable by one person, on a laptop, without dashboards or telemetry.\n\n## Filler patterns the new rules must catch\n\n1. **Target = baseline.** \"baseline 100%, target 100%\" — zero delta, no information.\n2. **Two KRs measuring the same dimension.** KR1 \"test pass rate ≥95%\" + KR3 \"test coverage ≥90%\" — both = \"tests are good.\"\n3. **KR is the Goal restated.** Goal: \"decision quality improves.\" KR: \"decision quality improves.\"\n4. **Sub-field points nowhere.** `source: the logs` / `baseline: currently unknown` / `target: better than today`.\n5. **Meta-KRs added for gate compliance.** \"We will document\" / \"we will write tests\" added to hit the count, not load-bearing pre-gate.\n\n## Layer 1 — the four personal-project dimensions (used by rule 5b)\n\n| Dimension | What it answers | When it's load-bearing |\n| -- | -- | -- |\n| **Correctness** | does the thing produce the intended output on the cases I care about | always, for any build |\n| **Outcome / behaviour change** | does the thing I built actually change what I do or how I work | most product-shaped initiatives (Types 2, 4, 5, 6 from `references/initiative-types.md`) |\n| **Maintenance / future-me cost** | can I come back in 30 days and still navigate this | always — solo means future-me pays the whole tax |\n| **Discipline / completion** | did the artefacts get finished, or did this half-ship | always — solo means nobody else will finish it |\n\nDropped from the original brainstormed list: performance, operability-as-SLO, adoption-as-funnel, calibration, coverage, no-silent-failure. They were enterprise-shaped sub-dimensions; they re-enter only as per-initiative exceptions when a specific initiative genuinely ships runtime traffic.\n\n## Layer 2 — measurement templates (one per Layer 1 dimension)\n\nLives in a new reference file `references/kr-quality-templates.md`, cited from rule 5 in the skill.\n\n| Layer 1 | Template | What a good KR includes | Example |\n| -- | -- | -- | -- |\n| **Correctness** | Golden-path test | named input(s) → named expected output → how it's verified (script run, snapshot diff, eyeball check) → judged-by date | \"Run `/initiative-shape` against 5 fixture inputs in `fixtures/`; all 5 produce the 6-field structure verified by a checked-in snapshot diff; judged at cycle close\" |\n| **Outcome** | Self-trial protocol | the next N times I hit situation X, observable behaviour Y happens (or doesn't); judged by inspecting the resulting artefacts | \"Next 5 initiatives shaped after this lands: zero filler KRs, judged by reading the Linear project descriptions\" |\n| **Maintenance** | Structural cap + revisit gate | size/shape cap (LOC, section count, file count) stays within a stated bound; revisit-at-N-days check that I can still navigate without re-reading | \"`SKILL.md` stays ≤ 300 lines; in 30 days I can answer 'where does rule 5c live' from memory\" |\n| **Discipline** | Artefact-exists + sections-complete | named artefact exists, named sections populated, no TBD/placeholder, predecessor/supersedes declared if applicable | \"<issue id=\"0b00aafe-b668-47db-8768-31eca82ad6f4\">ABA-191</issue> closes only after rule 5 has 5a/5b/5c/5d each with a 1-line pass condition, zero TBDs, and the rationalisations row added\" |\n\nEvery template above is verifiable by one person, locally, without instrumentation. That's the constraint.\n\n## Change — rubric rule #5\n\nReplace the current single-row rule with four sub-checks:\n\n* **5a. No degenerate KRs.** `target ≠ baseline` either numerically or by state change.\n* **5b. KRs name distinct dimensions.** No two KRs share the same Layer 1 dimension. Default vocabulary: correctness / outcome / maintenance / discipline (see `references/kr-quality-templates.md`). A KR may invoke an out-of-default dimension (e.g. performance) only when the initiative genuinely has that property.\n* **5c. KRs are not Goal-restatements.** Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it.\n* **5d. KR matches its Layer 2 template.** For each KR, name which Layer 1 dimension it sits in and confirm it matches the template in `references/kr-quality-templates.md`.\n* **5e. Existing language ban.** Keep the ban on \"improve\", \"better\", \"enhance\", and arbitrary count thresholds without a quality dimension.\n\n## Change — rubric rule #6\n\nExtend the placeholder ban list: also reject `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`. Require `source` to point to a concrete artefact: file path, directory, Linear query, log query, cached report URL, or metric name + system.\n\n## Change — rationalisations table\n\nAdd a row covering the \"I need a 3rd KR — let me add documentation / tests / logging\" pattern, with the rebuttal \"if the 3rd KR only exists to clear the gate, reshape or descope — don't pad.\"\n\n## New reference file — `references/kr-quality-templates.md`\n\nSingle new file. Contains:\n\n* The 4 Layer 1 dimensions with one-line definitions.\n* The 4 Layer 2 templates above, each with: when it applies, what fields it requires, one good example, one bad example.\n* A short \"out of scope for personal projects\" note naming the dimensions we deliberately don't use as defaults (performance, operability-as-SLO, adoption-as-funnel).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #5 enumerates 5a/5b/5c/5d/5e with concrete pass conditions.\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rubric rule #6 has the extended placeholder ban list and the concrete-artefact requirement for `source`.\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rationalisations table has the new \"padding to 3\" row.\n* Step 6.5 failure-report instruction names sub-rules (5a/5b/5c/5d/5e) when failing.\n* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` exists with the 4 Layer 1 dimensions + 4 Layer 2 templates + out-of-scope note.\n* Skill rule 5b and 5d cite `references/kr-quality-templates.md`.\n* Manual smoke test against each of the 5 filler patterns: re-run `/initiative-shape` with a draft that exhibits the pattern; confirm the gate rejects it with a pointer to the specific sub-rule.\n* Manual smoke test against a legitimate 3-KR draft (across all 4 Layer 1 dimensions): confirm it still passes.\n\n## Files\n\n* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` — rubric rules #5 and #6 in Step 6.5, plus the rationalisations table around lines 200-210.\n* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` — new file.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n* Changing rule #4 (count range stays `≥ 3 and ≤ 5`).\n* Changing the default KR count (still 3).\n* Changing type playbooks in `references/initiative-types.md`.\n* Edits to `rules/linear-workflow.md`.\n* Adding a \"waive the floor with justification\" override path.\n* Importing SRE SLO / DORA / AARRR vocabulary as defaults. They can be cited for a single initiative that genuinely needs them, but they are not the default Layer 1 vocabulary.\n\n## Background\n\nOriginally filed after the user hit filler-KR pain on recent projects (padding to 3 KRs just to clear the gate). <issue identifier="ABA-193">
<title>Make KRs grader-backed by default in /initiative-shape</title>
<description>
## Problem

KRs that require manual cycle-close inspection are expensive (an afternoon of reading), unreliable (verdict drifts with mood), and admit filler (you can write a KR that has no clear pass condition and nobody notices until the review is upon you).

A recent drain-cycle initiative demonstrated a stronger pattern: build the **grader** as part of the initiative itself, so the KR is verifiable by running a command rather than by reading artefacts.

## The pattern — grader-backed KR

A KR is **grader-backed** when its `source:` points at a tool (script, command, query) that reads the write-side artefacts and emits a verdict — not at the raw artefacts themselves.

Two-part shape, both halves are user stories *in the initiative*:

| Part | What it is |
| -- | -- |
| **Write-side artefact** | Every relevant event/run/state-change emits a structured record at a known location with a known schema. |
| **Read-side grader** | A command reads the write-side records and emits a verdict — numeric grade, OK/WATCH/KILL, or pass/fail. |

### Worked example (drain-cycle)

User stories from the drain-cycle initiative that exemplify the pattern:

> *As the operator, I want each drain run to write a structured log (issue order, spawn timestamps, exit codes, final Linear state) to* `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>.json` *— so KR1 (completion %) is gradable from the file alone, and KR2's time_spent block has a known home to be appended to at cycle close.*

> *As the operator, I want to run a command that reads the run logs across recent cycles and prints a grade — per-cycle completion %, trend across the last N cycles, recurrent failure-mode tuples, and a verdict against the kill condition (OK / WATCH / KILL) — so I can decide whether to ship-as-is, iterate, or walk away, without re-reading JSON by hand.*

## Why this is the right default

1. **Cheap to re-run.** Cycle close becomes one command, not an afternoon.
2. **Honest by construction.** If you have to write the grader, you can't fudge the KR. The grader returns a verdict whether you like it or not.
3. **Filler-KR filter.** Filler KRs don't admit graders. "We will document this" has no grader. "Skill exists and has all anatomy sections" does — `grep -c '^## ' SKILL.md` is the grader. **If you can't describe the grader command in one sentence, the KR is filler.**

That third property is the load-bearing one: it's a sharper rule than <issue id="0b00aafe-b668-47db-8768-31eca82ad6f4">ABA-191</issue>'s rule 5b/5c/5d combined.

## Layer 2 — each template gets a `grader:` sub-field

<issue id="0b00aafe-b668-47db-8768-31eca82ad6f4">ABA-191</issue> introduces 4 Layer 2 templates (golden-path test, self-trial protocol, structural cap + revisit, artefact-exists). This ticket extends each with a `grader:` sub-field, with a preferred shape:

| Layer 1 | Grader shape |
| -- | -- |
| **Correctness** | script that runs golden-path inputs and diffs against expected output → exit code + diff summary |
| **Outcome** | script that scans resulting artefacts (Linear issues, files, logs) and counts observable behaviour → numeric grade |
| **Maintenance** | one-liner that checks structural caps (`wc -l`, `grep -c`, file count) → pass/fail |
| **Discipline** | one-liner that asserts artefact existence + section count + no-TBD → pass/fail |

## Change — rubric rule #5

Extend rule 5d (introduced by <issue id="0b00aafe-b668-47db-8768-31eca82ad6f4">ABA-191</issue>) or add a new sub-rule:

* **5d (extended) — KR is grader-backed.** Each KR's `source:` points at a command/script/query that emits a verdict against the target. The grader command must be describable in one sentence at draft time. Building the grader (write-side schema + read-side command) is in the initiative's scope — included as one or two user stories — unless explicitly waived under the carve-out below.

### Manual-grading carve-out

A KR may be manually graded (no grader required) only when:

* The initiative is genuinely one-shot or throwaway (and the initiative type field reflects that), or
* The KR's verdict is irreducibly qualitative (e.g. "the prose reads naturally") and no automatable proxy is honest.

The carve-out is the exception, not the default. Drafts that lean on it without justification fail 5d.

## New section in `references/kr-quality-templates.md`

Add a section "The grader-backed KR pattern" containing:

* The two-part shape (write-side artefact + read-side grader).
* The drain-cycle worked example (verbatim user stories above).
* The one-sentence-grader test as the filler-KR filter.
* The four Layer 2 grader shapes (table above).
* The manual-grading carve-out.
* A short "building the grader is part of the initiative" subsection — names that grader user stories should appear in the issue list before the initiative enters Active.

## Acceptance criteria

* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` rule 5d extended (or new 5f added) requiring grader-backed KRs by default, with the manual-grading carve-out named.
* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` has a "Grader-backed KR pattern" section with the two-part shape, drain-cycle example, four Layer 2 grader shapes, and carve-out.
* Step 6.5 failure-report instruction recognises grader-related failures (5d).
* Smoke test: draft a KR with no grader — gate rejects with pointer to 5d.
* Smoke test: draft a KR with a one-sentence grader (e.g. "grader: `grep -c '^## ' SKILL.md` returns ≥ 11") — gate passes.

## Files

* `skills/initiative-shape/SKILL.md` — rule 5 extension in Step 6.5.
* `references/kr-quality-templates.md` — new section appended.

## Dependencies

* **Blocked by** <issue id="0b00aafe-b668-47db-8768-31eca82ad6f4">ABA-191</issue> — Layer 1/Layer 2 framework and `references/kr-quality-templates.md` must exist first.

## Out of scope

* Building actual graders for existing/in-flight initiatives (that's per-initiative work, not framework work).
* A schema standard for write-side artefacts (JSON vs JSONL vs SQLite is per-initiative).
* CI/automated enforcement of the rule (the rubric gate at Step 6.5 is the enforcement; CI is out of scope for the skill pack).
* Changes to `references/initiative-types.md` to bake graders into per-type playbooks (could be a follow-up).

## Why this is small

One rule extension in the skill, one section appended to a reference file. The pattern itself is already validated in the wild (drain-cycle).
</description>
